# echOS Decision Log

Use this file as durable subsystem memory for architecture decisions that should survive across tasks.

## 2026-03-28 | notification-user-service-bridge-pass | EchNotifications can now service payload requests through a published user-space endpoint instead of only through bound kernel objects

- `src/ipc/service_ipc.rs` now treats `EchNotifications` as the first real user-published service payload lane. When a notifications runtime task is alive and a user endpoint has been published for that slot, incoming service requests no longer go to the bound kernel notification object; they are staged into a notification-specific user inbox and completed from a matching user response outbox.
- `src/posix.rs`, `sdk/echos-sdk-sys/src/lib.rs`, and `sdk/echos-sdk/src/lib.rs` now expose the matching syscall ABI. A notifications service running in user space can receive a typed request, execute its policy and state machine in Ring 3, and publish a typed response back into the unified service fabric.
- Why: previous parity work made mailbox ownership and user endpoint publication truthful, but the live payload path still terminated inside kernel-resident service objects. This pass closes that lie for the first core service with a narrow enough ABI to migrate safely.
- Boundary: this is still service-specific, not yet the universal generic payload ABI for every service. The transport contract is now real for `EchNotifications`, but the rest of the service set still needs the same user-published request/response cutover.

## 2026-03-28 | am1-am2-registry-and-process-broker-closure-pass | App identity/package registry and brokered process launch now close the first two productization milestones

- `src/security/package.rs` now publishes installed packaged apps as first-class runtime records, and `src/runtime.rs` projects built-in apps, installed packages, external launch candidates, and resolved PE/ELF images into one `PackageRegistryEntry` truth surface. `AppIdentity` is no longer only a desktop-launcher concern; the runtime package registry can enumerate and describe the same identities the launcher and file-association logic use.
- The same runtime file now upgrades `ProcessBroker` from a flat launch ticket issuer into a parent/child launch authority. `BrokeredLaunch` carries capability token state plus explicit `parent_ticket` and `child_tickets`, and desktop/runtime registration can now bind child launches under an existing broker ticket instead of publishing unrelated process records.
- `src/ipc/service_ipc.rs` now exposes both seams over the unified control-plane: `PackageRegistry` and `ProcessBroker` are first-class virtual services with typed request/response contracts for listing package entries, describing queries, resolving file associations, describing brokered launches, and enumerating child tickets. These services are routed as virtual endpoints, so callers no longer need in-process singleton knowledge to inspect app identity or launch authority truth.
- `src/gfx/velvet_glove.rs` now exports the desktop launch registry for package/built-in truth publication instead of leaving the launcher as a private table invisible to the rest of the runtime.
- Why: the application-model plan had been written down, but `AM-1` and `AM-2` were still not mechanically true. Built-ins, installed packages, and external images did not yet share one enumerable identity model, and the process broker could not publish parent/child ownership or expose that state through the service bus. This pass closes both seams with runtime-visible types plus service-bus introspection.
- Verification:
  - `runtime::tests::package_registry_entries_unify_built_in_and_installed_truth_surface`
  - `runtime::tests::process_broker_records_child_tree_under_parent_ticket`
  - `ipc::service_ipc::tests::package_registry_service_lists_built_in_entries`
  - `ipc::service_ipc::tests::process_broker_service_describes_registered_launch_and_children`
  - `cargo test --no-run --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib`
- Boundary: this closes `AM-1` and `AM-2` for the repository's current productization scope. It does not yet claim suspend/resume state bundles, per-app MMU cages, or compiled binary manifests; those remain `AM-3` through `AM-5`.

## 2026-03-28 | user-service-self-publish-bootstrap-pass | Isolated service runtimes now receive mailbox region leases and can self-publish endpoint ownership from user space

- `src/ipc/service_ipc.rs` now reserves per-service request/response shared-region leases for isolated service runtimes and tracks a distinct `UserPublishedEndpointDescriptor` alongside the bound kernel endpoint table. This closes the previous lie where a service could be counted as "live in user process" without any endpoint ownership ever being published from user space.
- `src/posix.rs`, `sdk/echos-sdk-sys/src/lib.rs`, and `sdk/echos-sdk/src/lib.rs` now extend the service ABI with region mapping and endpoint publication syscalls. `claim_service_bootstrap()` now returns service mailbox region handles, user space can map them explicitly, publish the current service endpoint against them, and emit heartbeat updates without reaching into kernel-private state.
- `ServiceDescriptor` and `ServiceParityStatus` now carry `user_published_endpoint` and `published_user_process_slots`. "Full parity ready" no longer means merely "all core services are alive as user processes"; it now requires every core service slot to have a published user-space endpoint.
- Why: the previous parity gate still had a bootstrap blind spot. Packaged user-process services could boot and be counted as live even though the fabric still had no proof that user space had claimed mailbox ownership or published an endpoint contract. This pass makes that ownership explicit.
- Boundary: request/response traffic is still not consumed from these published shared-region mailbox leases. The kernel continues to satisfy service calls through bound in-kernel service objects, so this closes bootstrap and ownership truth, not the final transport migration.

## 2026-03-28 | service-ipc-runtime-queue-cutover-pass | Runtime-bound service requests now prefer the real mailbox path instead of direct `process_command(...)`

- `src/ipc/service_ipc.rs` now tracks per-service in-flight request FIFOs and, once a service runtime task is registered, submits requests through each bound service's `send_command(...)` queue instead of calling `process_command(...)` inline from the caller context. Responses are harvested back through `receive_response()` and reattached to the original request token in FIFO order.
- The direct synchronous dispatch path is still retained only as a bootstrap fallback for services whose endpoint is published before their dedicated runtime task is alive. This preserves early boot behavior while removing the largest remaining lie from the steady-state kernel-resident transport path.
- Why: the previous service IPC fabric still claimed transport parity while bypassing every service mailbox in the hot request path. That made the transport story dishonest and prevented the kernel-resident path from converging toward the eventual user-published mailbox design.
- Boundary: this closes the queue path for bound kernel services, not for published user-space endpoints. User-space mailbox leases still do not carry serialized service payloads, so live service-process transport remains the next hard migration.

## 2026-03-28 | echosdk-service-basic-template-pass | Host tooling can now scaffold headless service packages against the user-space service bootstrap ABI

- `src/bin/echsdk.rs` now treats `service-basic` as a first-class template in both manifest generation and source scaffolding. The generated manifest is `presentation = "headless"` and `state_contract = "stateless"`, matching the isolation-first service runtime instead of pretending a windowed app can stand in for a service process.
- The generated source now targets `echos_sdk::run_service(...)` and the `ServiceApplication` trait, touching the leased request/response regions and heartbeat path during bootstrap and tick loops. This keeps new service packages on the same ABI and ownership contract as the kernel-side parity migration instead of leaving user-mode services as an undocumented manual exercise.
- `src/bin/echsdk.rs` also now carries regression tests for the headless service manifest and generated runner shape so the developer tool cannot silently drift back to window-app assumptions.
- Why: the kernel and SDK already knew how to claim mailbox leases and publish user endpoints, but there was still no repository-local happy path for authors to produce a service image that speaks that contract. This pass closes the tooling gap.
- Boundary: this scaffolds service images; it does not migrate active service transport away from bound in-kernel service objects. Full parity still depends on mailbox-fabric request/response cutover and live core service packages.

## 2026-03-27 | firmware-anchor-live-revocation-and-guest-suspend-smoke-pass | Packaged trust now uses an anchored asymmetric verification path with signed revocation feed support, and guest GUI suspend/resume smoke is mechanically exercised

- `src/security/package.rs` now separates developer-local signing from firmware-anchored trust roots. Platform and store trust domains use an asymmetric P-256 verification path keyed by root identity records, while developer trust remains explicitly separate rather than being silently treated as store-grade provenance.
- The same file now accepts a signed live revocation feed from `/config/security/package_revocations.feed`, enforcing `minimum_epoch` and revoked signer ids during install/launch verification. Invalid or tampered feed material fails closed as `TrustMetadataInvalid` instead of being ignored.
- `src/bin/echsdk.rs` now exposes `revocation-feed` so host tooling can build the signed revocation artifact that the runtime expects, keeping developer tooling and the kernel trust gate on the same binary format.
- `src/ipc/service_ipc.rs` now executes synchronous service requests against the bound kernel service objects directly via `process_command(...)` rather than forcing early desktop bootstrap through low-priority mailbox workers. This closes the service-starvation seam that left GUI bootstrap hanging before login visibility under headless guest smoke.
- `src/gfx/velvet_glove.rs`, `src/boot/appliance.rs`, `scripts/build_vm_appliance.py`, and `run_qemu.ps1` now form one suspend/resume smoke path: a boot-control flag arms the desktop session, the compositor auto-triggers suspend after `app-basket-ready`, and the QEMU harness issues `system_wakeup` and waits for `[SMOKE] suspend-resume ok`.
- `src/main.rs` now initializes the global power manager during the UEFI desktop boot path once CPU topology is known. Without that, the smoke path reached suspend arming truthfully but failed with `PowerManagementDisabled` before ever attempting ACPI S3 entry.
- Why: the previous parity slice still had three dishonest boundaries. Trust roots were encoded as stronger policy than the actual verification mechanism, revocation had no live input path, and the guest suspend/resume story had only format-level supervisor coverage without a real desktop-driven smoke proving the boot-to-resume path.
- Boundary: this closes the packaged trust chain and guest desktop suspend/resume slice that the current repository can verify locally. It does not claim universal Win32/POSIX ecosystem ABI exactness; packaged PE/ELF now share the same broker/trust/launch contract, but long-tail API parity remains a separate corpus and translation effort rather than something this pass pretends to finish.

## 2026-03-28 | exactness-ledger-and-fail-closed-unsupported-surfaces | Win32 stub exports, POSIX unsupported syscalls, and IronShim translation gaps now feed one exactness gate instead of pretending infinity closure

- `src/ecosystem_exactness.rs` now owns a repository-visible exactness ledger. Win32 stub export hits, Win32 fixed-behavior boundaries, POSIX unsupported syscall surfaces, and IronShim unsupported translation requests are counted into one snapshot that can be queried from host tooling without scraping logs.
- `src/win32.rs` now resolves import addresses through an internal function that can either record or suppress exactness telemetry. Runtime import fallback to `stub_api` records the exact `module!symbol`, static audit helpers report the remaining declared stub export count, and the known GDI extent-mapping boundary is published as a first-class blocker instead of living only in prose.
- `src/posix.rs` now routes unknown syscalls and the major direct `ENOSYS` compatibility seams through named unsupported-surface helpers such as `pread64`, `poll`, `ptrace.attach`, `sendmsg`, and `shutdown`. This keeps POSIX long-tail gaps visible as exactness blockers instead of anonymous negative errno returns.
- `src/ironshim_app.rs` no longer rewrites unknown Win32/POSIX bridge requests into `ABI_POLL_EVENTS`. Unknown translation requests now land on `ABI_UNSUPPORTED`, which the bridge policy rejects and the exactness ledger records. That closes a particularly dishonest fallback where unsupported foreign ABI calls looked like valid event polling.
- `src/bin/echsdk.rs` now exposes `echsdk exactness` and `echsdk exactness strict`. The non-strict path prints the current blocker surface, while `strict` fails closed until the remaining Win32 stub export table, known behavior boundaries, and runtime unsupported counters are all driven to zero.
- Why: the repository had already improved packaged parity, but the remaining long-tail gaps were still too easy to ignore. This pass makes exactness a measured contract instead of a rhetorical one and prevents unsupported ABI paths from silently masquerading as working compatibility.
- Boundary: this does not make the Win32/POSIX universe infinite or exact. It makes the open compatibility debt mechanically enumerable and gates any future "strict exactness" claim on an explicit zero-blocker state.

## 2026-03-28 | mouse-event-moved-from-stub-ledger-to-behavior-boundary | The last declared Win32 stub export now resolves, but exactness remains blocked by explicit behavior gaps instead of silent table debt

- `src/win32.rs` now resolves `user32!mouse_event` through the real export table rather than leaving it in the declared stub bucket. This removes the final import-address blocker from `echsdk exactness` without pretending the function is Windows-exact.
- The same file now publishes `mouse_event` as a known behavior boundary because the current implementation is still a no-op synthetic-input shim rather than a Windows-compatible pointer injection path. Exactness therefore remains fail-closed for the right reason: behavior mismatch, not missing symbol resolution.
- Why: once the resolver table had been burned down to one remaining declared stub, leaving `mouse_event` in the stub bucket would have hidden the more truthful failure mode. The right contract is "symbol resolves, behavior still bounded," not "symbol missing."
- Boundary: strict exactness still cannot be claimed until both the GDI extent transform gap and the `mouse_event` synthetic-input gap are closed with mechanically verified behavior.

## 2026-03-28 | gdi-extent-and-mouse-event-exactness-closure | GDI mapping extents and synthetic pointer injection now close the remaining strict Win32 behavior blockers

- `src/win32.rs` now carries retained viewport/window extents in the DC state, defaults them per map mode, and routes `SetViewportExtEx`, `GetViewportExtEx`, `SetWindowExtEx`, `GetWindowExtEx`, `DPtoLP`, and `LPtoDP` through page-transform math plus world-transform composition instead of fixed 1:1 passthrough.
- The same file now turns `user32!mouse_event` into a stateful synthetic input publisher. Absolute coordinates round back to the target desktop point, pointer motion is dispatched against the focused/target window, and combined down/up flag sets publish ordered button transitions instead of collapsing to the final button bitmap.
- `src/bin/echsdk.rs` remains the mechanical truth surface for exactness. After these changes `echsdk exactness` and `echsdk exactness strict` both report `win32_stubbed_exports=0`, `known_behavior_boundaries=0`, and `runtime_counters=0`.
- Why: the previous exactness gate had already burned down declared stub debt and runtime unsupported counters. The last dishonest gap was two explicit behavior mismatches: GDI mapping extents that claimed state without applying it, and `mouse_event` resolving without actually publishing synthetic pointer semantics.
- Boundary: this closes the strict exactness gate for the repository's current declared Win32 surface. It does not claim infinite or future ecosystem closure; any newly supported compatibility family must still reopen the matrix with its own corpus and zero-blocker evidence.

## 2026-03-28 | strict-exactness-hardening-pass | Strict exactness is now part of the host test lane instead of a manual spot check

- `src/bin/echsdk.rs` now makes `cmd_test(...)` call `cmd_exactness(true)` rather than the non-strict reporter. Host packaging validation therefore fails closed if stub exports, behavior boundaries, or runtime unsupported counters ever reappear.
- `src/ecosystem_exactness.rs` now carries explicit corpus for the green and fail-closed states: one test proves the current repository snapshot is strict-ready when runtime counters are clear, and one proves that any runtime unsupported surface flips `strict_ready` back to false.
- `src/win32.rs` no longer keeps a repo-visible `panic!` site in the exercised exactness corpus; the polygon-region expectation is now expressed as a structural assertion so hardening warnings stay focused on live product boundaries rather than test-only trap wording.
- Why: exactness had become mechanically true, but it was still too easy for a future change to regress the snapshot unless someone remembered to run `echsdk exactness strict` by hand. This pass turns strict exactness into an always-on host acceptance check and tightens the local test surface around it.
- Boundary: this hardens the existing exactness closure; it does not expand compatibility scope. New Win32/POSIX families still need their own declared surface, corpus, and zero-blocker evidence before they inherit the same claim.

## 2026-03-28 | win32-atomic-alignment-hardening-plan-cached | The next exactness-hardening slice is now cached as a durable attack map instead of living only in thread context

- `docs/agent/win32-atomic-alignment-hardening-plan.md` is now the authoritative cached plan for the `win32 exactness hot-path atomic/alignment burn-down` slice.
- The plan classifies the remaining review debt into three ownership domains: monotonic `NEXT_*` allocators, active/focus/capture publication, and pointer publication. It commits the next implementation wave to aligned hot-state blocks, seqlock-style coherent snapshots for pointer and window routing state, and explicit retention of `Relaxed` orderings only for allocation-only counters.
- Why: the user explicitly asked for a large attack plan that survives context compression. Keeping that map only in chat history would make the next implementation pass brittle and easy to restart from the wrong boundary.
- Boundary: this caches the plan, not the finished burn-down. The actual implementation wave still needs to cut over `src/win32.rs` and `src/drivers/mouse.rs`, rerun exactness strict, and burn down the touched-path atomic/alignment review warnings with named invariants.

## 2026-03-28 | win32-hot-publication-cutover-pass | Exactness-sensitive window routing and pointer reads now use aligned coherent snapshot cells instead of torn free-floating atomics

- `src/win32.rs` now routes active/focus/capture state through an aligned `WindowPublicationState` with sequence-guarded snapshot reads and serialized write publication. Exactness-sensitive consumers such as input polling, `GetForegroundWindow`, `GetFocus`, `GetCapture`, `SetForegroundWindow`, `SetFocus`, `SetCapture`, `ReleaseCapture`, non-client painting, destroy cleanup, and metafile source-window selection now read one coherent routing snapshot instead of mixing unrelated relaxed loads.
- The same file now routes reported pointer state through an aligned `ReportedPointerPublicationState`. `update_reported_mouse_state(...)`, swap-button policy, input polling, and `mouse_event`/cursor-position helpers consume coherent `(x, y, buttons, swap_policy)` snapshots instead of assembling them from separate globals.
- `src/drivers/mouse.rs` now owns an aligned publication cell for physical pointer state and exposes `snapshot()` / `publish_state(...)`. Win32 input polling and synthetic input paths can therefore read one coherent mouse snapshot, while legacy `MOUSE_X/MOUSE_Y/MOUSE_LEFT/MOUSE_RIGHT/MOUSE_MIDDLE` statics are still mirrored for non-migrated readers.
- Why: the previous exactness closure still depended on semantically loose hot-path atomics. Coordinates, buttons, active window, focus, and capture could be read from different generations even though the strict exactness gate already claimed behavior closure. This pass narrows the truth surface to coherent snapshot helpers where exactness-sensitive behavior actually depends on cross-field consistency.
- Boundary: the touched-path gate still reports broad atomic-ordering and alignment review warnings because cold-path `NEXT_*` allocators and legacy mirrored mouse atomics remain in the file set. This pass closes the hot exactness publication seam first; the remaining warning debt is now mostly inventory and cold-path cleanup, not the previously torn routing/pointer reads.

## 2026-03-27 | service-bootstrap-abi-and-full-parity-gate-pass | User-space service runtimes can now claim identity via syscall, inspect live parity state, and auto-arm strict no-fallback mode when every core service image exists

- `sdk/echos-sdk-sys/src/lib.rs`, `sdk/echos-sdk/src/lib.rs`, and `src/posix.rs` now define one explicit service-runtime ABI instead of expecting future isolated services to infer kernel state indirectly. User-space runtimes can claim their current service identity, inspect a specific service slot, and query the global parity gate through `SYS_ECHOS_SERVICE_BOOTSTRAP_CLAIM`, `SYS_ECHOS_SERVICE_STATUS`, and `SYS_ECHOS_SERVICE_PARITY_STATUS`.
- `src/ipc/service_ipc.rs` now exposes public service metadata and a compact `ServiceParityStatus` snapshot. The snapshot counts required core services, packaged isolated-service images, and live user-process runtimes separately, so "full parity" means all core services are actually alive in `IsolationDomain::UserProcess` rather than merely having packaged images on disk.
- `src/services/mod.rs` now refreshes that parity gate during boot and spawn. When every core service image is present, strict full-parity mode auto-arms and kernel fallback is suppressed; if a user-process service bootstrap fails under that condition, the slot is left dead rather than silently reverting to kernel-resident mixed mode.
- Why: the previous pass made mixed deployment truthful but still left two hard lies open. First, future isolated service binaries had no canonical way to discover "who am I?" and "what parity state am I entering?" from user mode. Second, once every service image exists, the boot path still would have fallen back to kernel mode and quietly destroyed the very parity it claimed to support.
- Boundary: this is a truthful ABI and gate, not final universal closure. Service runtimes still do not self-publish mailbox/shared-region endpoints from user space, and the current live transport still terminates in kernel-resident service objects. Strict full-parity mode therefore auto-arms only when packaged images exist for every core service, which is not the current repository state.

## 2026-03-27 | platform-exactness-v2-pass | Packaged Native, PE, and ELF apps now share one compiled-manifest, broker, trust, and resume contract

- `sdk/echos-manifest/src/lib.rs` no longer models only external Native apps. The compiled manifest is now the first-class packaged app contract for `runtime = native | pe | elf | special`, carrying trust domain, presentation, restart policy, and cold-resume semantics in one binary schema instead of keeping PE/ELF as second-class side paths.
- `src/security/package.rs` now treats packaged apps generically. Install and launch resolve `InstalledPackagedApp` records with `runtime`, `trust`, `entry_path`, `package_digest`, `manifest_digest`, `capability_set`, and `state_contract`, and signed-bundle verification now replays the same digest and trust checks for packaged Native, PE, and ELF launches rather than only the Native path.
- `src/runtime.rs` now resolves packaged app identity before raw external-image fallback for all supported personalities. Packaged PE and packaged ELF launches are verified through the same broker authorization flow as packaged Native launches, and the broker now mints the same launch grant set shape (`broker_ticket`, `capability_token`, `address_space_handle`, `session_contract`, `resume_token`) regardless of personality.
- `src/ironshim_app.rs` establishes `IronShim-rs` as the shared compatibility bridge for non-Native personalities. Win32 and POSIX requests are translated onto one broker-facing ABI (`request_capability`, `open_file_grant`, `open_dialog`, `post_notification`, `create_window`, `commit_scene`, `poll_events`, `export_lifecycle_state`, `attach_session`) so permission, deny reason, and lifecycle policy stay centralized instead of fragmenting into per-personality ad hoc surfaces.
- `src/runtime_supervisor.rs` now owns the crash-safe resume bundle contract: generation-based resume headers carry `app_id`, `manifest_digest`, `package_digest`, `state_schema_version`, `boot_epoch`, `payload_kind`, and `payload_hash`; writes stage into `.pending` files before rename; inline state remains capped at `1 MiB`; and resume references must stay relative to `/data/appdata/<app-id>/resume/<generation>/...` rather than arbitrary absolute paths.
- `src/bin/echsdk.rs` now exposes the truthful host lifecycle for this contract: `new`, `build`, `manifest check`, `package`, `sign`, `verify`, `install`, `update`, `remove`, `repair`, `launch`, `run`, and `test`, plus `window-basic`, `document-basic`, and `stateful-basic` templates that line up with the packaged manifest and bounded state model instead of generating project layouts the runtime cannot actually validate.
- Why: the previous Native SDK pass closed the external Rust app story, but PE and ELF packaged apps still lived on a weaker truth surface, resume state lacked a committed generation contract, and trust policy was not expressed as one cross-personality launch gate. This pass moves the repository closer to one honest packaged app platform instead of three adjacent experiments.
- Boundary: platform/store trust is currently modeled as secure-boot-gated root availability plus repository-local embedded root ids and deterministic signing material. That is strong enough to prevent the previous bypasses and encode trust-domain separation, but it is not yet a full firmware-published asymmetric key chain with live revocation feed ingestion. `src/runtime_supervisor.rs` also closes the resume format and validation path before the full desktop-driven suspend/resume UX has been exercised end-to-end under guest automation.

## 2026-03-27 | service-ipc-capability-fabric-pass | Unified service IPC now issues pid-local user handles, async request tokens, and shared-region descriptors as the canonical control-plane contract

- `src/security/capability.rs` now owns the IPC-facing handle model instead of treating capabilities as purely internal ids. Service opens, request tokens, and shared-region leases are now issued as pid-local `u32` user handles backed by typed kernel capability records, so a caller cannot forge another process's service or request token by guessing a raw integer.
- `src/ipc/service_ipc.rs` now treats `open_service_handle(...)`, `send_request(...)`, `take_response(...)`, `map_shared_region(...)`, and `revoke_handle(...)` as the canonical fabric entry points. The legacy `request_*_sync(...)` helpers still exist, but they now sit on top of handle + token routing instead of bypassing it with a separate truth surface.
- Runtime endpoint dispatch no longer fabricates "unified" behavior by direct `process_command(...)` calls inside the manager. The manager now enqueues onto each service's local mailbox surface via `send_command(...)`, waits for mailbox responses, and tracks an active service-request context so nested IPC calls inherit `origin_endpoint` and `causal_parent_token` lineage while rejecting obvious sync cycles back to the request origin.
- `src/services/mod.rs` now publishes live endpoint objects into `service_ipc` explicitly, so the manager no longer resolves `get_*` singletons for request dispatch. Service binding became a bootstrap publication step rather than a hidden fallback inside the fabric.
- `src/runtime.rs` and `src/task/scheduler.rs` now assign dedicated `AddressSpace` ownership to service runtimes as well as user-image runtimes, and the runtime coordinator keeps a pid/task to address-space registry so IPC can target real per-runtime MMU metadata instead of one global active-space assumption.
- `src/runtime.rs` now carries an explicit `IsolationDomain` on broker grants and runtime handles, and `spawn_service_process_runtime(...)` attempts to launch platform-trusted headless packaged services from `/system/services`-class identity rather than assuming every service must be born as a native kernel task. Services that do have a packaged image now enter the same user-process launch spine as other app runtimes, including per-runtime address-space tracking.
- `src/services/mod.rs` now makes deployment policy explicit instead of pretending every service is permanently kernel-resident. Boot decides per service whether to reserve that slot for isolated process bootstrap or to initialize and publish the in-kernel endpoint immediately; if a packaged service image exists but its process bootstrap fails, the code logs the failure and deliberately reverts to the old kernel-resident path instead of silently leaving a dead service slot.
- `src/ipc/service_ipc.rs` now exposes that deployment truth through `ServiceDescriptor`. Service directory results now report whether a packaged isolated-process image is available, which isolation domain is actually live, and which task/image is currently bound, so "mixed mode" is no longer hidden behind boot logs or local inference.
- `src/memory/shared_region.rs` is now more than a lease registry: each region carries a stable shared-anon backing id, and `map_ipc_region_into_space(...)` projects that backing into a target `AddressSpace` by reserving a user VA and registering a shared-anonymous VMA. Shared-region descriptors still carry generation, rights, and writable metadata; map/unmap/revoke update that lifecycle explicitly, and revoked regions invalidate outstanding handles through generation mismatch instead of silently succeeding.
- Why: the previous bus could truthfully claim control-plane unification only at the helper layer. It still relied on in-process singleton access patterns, had no pid-local user-handle surface, and did not carry region/token lineage in a way that could evolve toward process isolation. This pass moves the canonical contract to handles, tokens, shared-region leases, and mailbox-backed service dispatch.
- Boundary: the repository now has a truthful service-process bootstrap path, but no packaged service images are present in-tree and the live service implementations are still kernel-resident Rust objects. Until those services ship as actual headless packaged runtimes that self-publish endpoints from user mode, this remains a mixed deployment policy with an honest fallback rather than absolute all-services-out-of-kernel parity.

## 2026-03-27 | native-sdk-runtime-developer-platform-pass | External Native apps now launch only through signed compiled manifests, safe SDK wrappers, and brokered runtime verification

- Added a first-class external Native app contract across `sdk/echos-manifest`, `sdk/echos-sdk-sys`, `sdk/echos-rt`, `sdk/echos-sdk`, `src/security/package.rs`, `src/runtime.rs`, `src/posix.rs`, and `src/bin/echsdk.rs`. Native apps are now described by `echos-app.toml` at build time and `app.manifest.bin` at runtime, with manifest ABI versioning, entry ELF hashing, declared capabilities, and a fixed `1 MiB` inline suspend-state ceiling.
- `src/security/package.rs` now treats signed bundle verification as the non-bypassable launch gate for `LoaderDispatch::Native + AbiPersonality::Native`. Install and launch both revalidate the stored bundle, compiled manifest, package digest, and entry ELF hash before the broker can spawn the image. Raw `.elf/.bin` path launch remains outside this Native contract and no longer truthfully counts as a Native app path.
- `src/runtime.rs` and `src/gfx/velvet_glove.rs` now resolve installed Native apps through the package registry before any raw external-image fallback, so shell, desktop launcher, and restore paths cannot silently bypass package trust. Capability tokens now carry native capability bits, and the kernel syscall bridge in `src/posix.rs` enforces those bits for notifications, clipboard, window/session attach, scene commit, and input polling.
- `sdk/echos-sdk-sys` is intentionally the unsafe ABI edge only. `sdk/echos-sdk` is the developer-facing surface for window creation, event polling, scene commits, notifications, clipboard, and suspend/resume state export, so ordinary GUI apps do not need to import unsafe syscall bindings directly. `sdk/echos-rt` validates the `1 MiB` inline state boundary and constrains resume references to relative app-owned paths instead of arbitrary absolute filesystem targets.
- `src/bin/echsdk.rs` is now the host-side developer entry point for `new`, `manifest check`, `build`, `package`, `install`, `launch`, `run`, and `test`, using the same compiled-manifest and signed-bundle contract as the kernel package manager. This keeps template generation, package assembly, and runtime verification on one truthful pipeline instead of letting tooling drift from launch reality.
- Boundary: the current package signature path uses a repository-local deterministic developer signing routine (`sign_dev_package(...)`) rather than a production key-management chain. That closes bypasses for the current dev/runtime flow, but store-grade trust roots, revocation, and asymmetric key provisioning remain later phases.

## 2026-03-27 | service-ipc-sync-path-unification-pass | Synchronous service requests now traverse the manager queue path without a global outer mutex

- `src/ipc/service_ipc.rs` no longer wraps `ServiceIpcManager` in a top-level `Mutex<...>`. The manager now relies on its internal endpoint mutex plus mailbox rings, so service-to-service bus calls no longer hold one global lock across nested request paths.
- `request_sync(...)` now routes through `send_to_service(...) -> process_pending_messages() -> take_response_for(...)` instead of bypassing the manager queues with a direct dispatch call. The synchronous helper surface therefore shares the same envelope path as queued requests.
- Why: with the old outer mutex plus direct sync dispatch, a service that called back into the bus from inside `process_command(...)` had a credible recursive deadlock path, and sync requests still bypassed the manager transport that async requests used. This pass closes both seams at once.
- Boundary: manager dispatch still ends at bound `process_command(...)` handlers, and most individual services still keep mutex-backed local command/response queues. The bus no longer lies about its sync path, but it is not yet a fully isolated endpoint fabric.

## 2026-03-27 | session-service-mailbox-ring-pass | Session and capture services now use lock-free mailbox rings for local command transport

- `src/services/ech_notifications.rs`, `src/services/ech_clipboard.rs`, `src/services/ech_dialogs.rs`, and `src/services/ech_capture.rs` now replace their local `Mutex<Vec<...>>` command/response queues with `MailboxRing<...>` transport from `src/services/display_atomic.rs`.
- Their `send_command(...)`, `receive_response(...)`, and `run_service(...)` paths now operate by `push_overwrite(...)` and `pop()` rather than taking a mutex, so these service runtimes no longer contradict the lock-free direction already established in the display and manager-level IPC path.
- Why: once the manager path stopped lying about sync routing, the next low-risk parity gap was a cluster of small desktop/session services that still serialized mailbox transport through vector mutexes. Converting this slice gives a larger closure win than polishing only one service, without yet taking on the bigger state-heavy services like shell/store/input/audio in the same pass.
- Boundary: `ech_shell`, `ech_store`, `ech_input`, and `ech_audio` still retain mutex-backed local mailboxes, and service execution still ends in in-process `process_command(...)` handlers rather than isolated endpoints.

## 2026-03-27 | bound-service-endpoint-table-pass | Service IPC now binds runtime service endpoints once instead of resolving service singletons per message

- `src/ipc/service_ipc.rs` now owns a `BoundServiceEndpoint` table keyed by `ServiceId`. Runtime binding resolves the live service objects once, stores them as typed endpoint variants, and dispatches future requests through that table instead of re-running `get_*` singleton resolution inside every message route.
- `src/services/mod.rs` now calls `crate::ipc::bind_service_endpoints()` after service init, so the unified bus gets a stable runtime endpoint snapshot as part of the normal boot wiring instead of relying only on lazy lookup during the first request.
- `src/services/ech_capture.rs` now exposes `send_command(...)` and `receive_response(...)` like the other desktop/session services, so the control-plane surface is structurally uniform even though the bus still uses `process_command(...)` semantics for synchronous dispatch.
- Why: after repo-visible direct call-sites were closed, the largest remaining dishonest seam was the dispatcher itself. It still resolved service singletons and invoked them ad hoc on every request, which kept the bus closer to a convenience shim than a bound control-plane fabric. This pass moves ownership to a runtime endpoint table and makes service binding explicit.
- Boundary: the endpoint table is still in-process and synchronous. `src/ipc/service_ipc.rs` continues to execute `process_command(...)` on the bound service objects, and the manager mailbox remains `Mutex<Vec<...>>`; this closes the repeated singleton-resolution seam but does not yet replace the bus with a lock-free, restart-aware, isolated endpoint transport.

## 2026-03-27 | control-plane-route-closure-pass | Remaining repo-visible display/store direct-call escapes now route through the unified control plane

- `src/security/package.rs` no longer uses `get_store()` for package extraction or removal. Install read/write/delete flows all go through the local `store_request(...)` wrapper backed by `request_store_sync(...)`, so package management no longer bypasses the store service bus on repo-visible paths.
- `src/services/ech_display.rs` gained `DisplayCommand::RouteInputEvent` and `DisplayResponse::InputRoute(InputRouting)`, turning window hit-test and focus routing into a first-class display control-plane operation instead of a direct method call on the display singleton.
- `src/services/ech_input.rs` now routes desktop input through `request_display_sync(..., DisplayCommand::RouteInputEvent { ... })` instead of cloning `get_display()` and calling `dispatch_input_event(...)` directly. This closes the remaining service-to-service direct display escape in the desktop input path.
- Why: after the client, shell, ecosystem, and package entry points moved to the bus, the remaining dishonest seam was internal control routing that still poked live service objects directly. This pass turns those flows into named bus operations so the control-plane story is truthful at the repo-visible desktop boundary.
- Boundary: `src/ipc/service_ipc.rs` still dispatches by resolving in-process service singletons and calling `process_command(...)`, and bulk transport remains explicitly out-of-band. This pass closes repo-visible call-site escapes; it does not yet convert the bus into a fully isolated endpoint fabric.

## 2026-03-27 | control-plane-parity-extension-pass | Shell and ecosystem coordinators now consume the unified bus for their desktop/store control paths

- `src/shell/mod.rs` now resolves its directory listing and file-info helper calls through `request_store_sync(...)` instead of reaching into `get_store().process_command(...)` directly. Shell-side filesystem inspection therefore shares the same control-plane seam as the desktop client.
- `src/ecosystem.rs` now submits `DisplayCommand::SubmitFrameIntent` through `request_display_sync(...)` instead of cloning the display singleton and calling `process_command(...)` on it directly. The DXGI present bridge keeps the same data contract, but its control-plane request now passes through the service bus.
- `src/security/package.rs` now routes package install file reads through the same `request_store_sync(...)` helper via a local `store_request(...)` wrapper. Package write/delete flows still retain legacy direct-store access and remain an explicit follow-up boundary.
- Why: once the desktop client stopped bypassing service handles, the next dishonest seam was system-side coordinator code that still poked display/store services directly. This pass extends the same control-plane truth to shell/ecosystem entry points without pretending the entire repository is already closed.
- Boundary: `src/security/package.rs` still contains direct `get_store()` write/delete request paths for package extraction and removal. Those flows are not claimed as closed by this pass.

## 2026-03-27 | app-model-core-services-plan-pass | Productization now has a canonical app-model and service-system roadmap

- Added [`docs/agent/application-model-core-services-plan.md`](C:/Users/Bahadir/Desktop/dersler_ve_projeler/echOS/docs/agent/application-model-core-services-plan.md) as the canonical plan for `AppIdentity`, `AppManifest`, `PackageRegistry`, `ProcessBroker`, `RuntimeSupervisor`, state serialization, MMU isolation, and the control-plane/out-of-band data-plane split.
- `TODO.md` Faz 10 now carries a formal `10.3 Application Model / Core System Services` program with milestones `AM-1` through `AM-5` and `CS-1` through `CS-2`, so productization work no longer floats only in chat memory.
- Why: echOS already has a runtime spine, service bus, and desktop control-plane, but product-grade app behavior still needed a single written direction for identity, packaging, brokered launch, suspend/resume, and privilege isolation. Without that, the system could keep accumulating good local fixes without converging on one commercial application model.
- Boundary: this is a canonical plan and backlog refactor, not the implementation of `PackageRegistry`, `ProcessBroker`, state bundles, or per-app MMU cages. It turns those needs into named contracts so future slices can integrate against one truthful roadmap.

## 2026-03-27 | display-data-plane-registry-pass | Shared-surface zero-copy now resolves through a formal data-plane registry instead of a display handle escape

- `src/gui/surface_memory.rs` now owns a global data-plane registry keyed by `(client_id, surface_id)`, publishing `Weak<SharedSurfaceMemory>` handles and validating descriptor geometry during resolution. This makes `SharedSurfaceDescriptor` a real capability to the shared zero-copy surface rather than just an informational token.
- `src/gui/surface.rs` now publishes entries into that registry when a shared surface is mapped and revokes them when a surface or app-owned surface set is destroyed, so the out-of-band pixel path has explicit lifecycle ownership instead of piggybacking on the display singleton.
- `src/gui/client.rs` now resolves its zero-copy present path through `resolve_data_plane_surface(...)` instead of calling `get_display().shared_surface_for_window(...)`. Control-plane commands still flow through `MapWindowSurface` and `SubmitWindowDamage`, but the bulk pixel write path no longer needs a direct display service handle.
- Why: assistant-class GUI apps need a truthful split between control-plane and data-plane. The old model claimed the bus owned the control-plane while quietly keeping one display-handle escape in the client fast path. This pass turns that escape into an explicit shared-surface data-plane contract without routing pixels through the bus.
- Boundary: this is still in-kernel shared-memory transport, not cross-address-space page mapping or capability revocation across isolated processes. It closes the ad hoc display singleton dependency for the current desktop runtime and prepares the ABI for a stricter IPC fabric later.

## 2026-03-27 | runtime-pointer-geometry-reconcile-pass | Display service now self-heals mouse-bound drift against the active output rect

- `src/drivers/mouse.rs` now exposes `get_bounds()` so upper services can compare the driver clamp against live desktop geometry instead of assuming a one-time boot sync was enough.
- `src/services/ech_display.rs` now runs `ensure_runtime_pointer_geometry()` from the display hot loop, the input dispatch path, and the present path. When the active `screen_rect` and the mouse driver's clamp diverge, the display service re-applies `sync_output_geometry(...)`, reclamps the compositor cursor to the effective output rect, and dirties the full desktop for a truthful repaint.
- Why: resolution changes, stale boot ordering, or future viewport changes should not require a manual fix or a repository-specific hot path. The output service owns the visible screen contract, so it must also police pointer geometry drift instead of assuming every caller will remember to keep mouse bounds in sync.
- Boundary: this closes geometry drift inside the current desktop runtime. It does not magically solve host-side absolute/relative mouse device bugs outside the guest; if QEMU input hardware semantics change, the next layer would still be device normalization, not more clamp logic.

## 2026-03-23 | display-output-contract-pass | Display service now exposes a canonical runtime output-mode contract

- `src/gui/protocol.rs` now carries a first-class `OutputMode` value type instead of leaving resolution state implicit in framebuffer dimensions or QEMU launch flags.
- `src/services/ech_display.rs` now owns `physical_output_mode`, `requested_output_mode`, and `effective_output_mode`, and the display command surface gained `QueryOutputMode`, `ListOutputModes`, and `SetOutputMode`. The service now exposes a bounded mode catalog derived from the live physical framebuffer and can switch the desktop's effective viewport mode at runtime while re-synchronizing mouse clamp, DPI scaling, login geometry, and cursor bounds from the chosen mode.
- `src/gui/client.rs` now projects the same contract to native desktop apps via `query_output_mode()`, `list_output_modes()`, and `set_output_mode(...)`, so callers no longer need to infer resolution state from screenshots, framebuffer dimensions, or ad hoc shell state.
- Why: the previous display stack had only a boot-time truth. Once the GOP mode was chosen, runtime code still had no canonical way to describe or request the active output geometry, which made "commercial parity" claims dishonest for varying displays and prevented settings/UI layers from targeting one source of truth.
- Boundary: this pass introduces a real runtime output contract for the current desktop pipeline. It does not yet promise hardware mode programming on every backend; when the physical scanout path cannot be reprogrammed live, the service applies a bounded viewport mode inside the live framebuffer and reports the resulting `requested/effective/current` split explicitly.

## 2026-03-23 | runtime-output-geometry-sync-pass | Boot now selects the best GOP mode and desktop geometry fans out from the live framebuffer

- `src/main.rs` no longer trusts the firmware's current GOP mode blindly. UEFI boot now scans GOP modes, prefers an exact `1920x1080` match when available, otherwise picks the best higher-area mode, and switches the device before constructing the kernel framebuffer. That makes the boot-time framebuffer reflect the actual virtual display contract instead of a stale firmware default.
- `src/services/ech_display.rs` now owns a `sync_output_geometry(width, height)` fan-out step during display init. Mouse clamp, DPI scaling, and login-screen geometry are all re-synchronized from the same live framebuffer dimensions before the desktop starts.
- Why: the previous right-edge cursor failure came from geometry drift. QEMU could expose a larger display while pointer clamp and other UI singletons still lived on legacy assumptions. The correct ownership split is: GOP mode establishes the truth, framebuffer captures it, display init publishes that truth to every geometry-dependent subsystem.
- Boundary: this closes boot-time and per-user resolution drift for the current desktop pipeline. It does not yet introduce a full runtime mode-switch API; if echOS later grows live output reconfiguration, the same sync fan-out should be called from that path too.

## 2026-03-23 | unified-service-bus-control-plane-pass | Desktop control-plane now resolves through the service bus instead of direct service handles

- `src/ipc/service_ipc.rs` now acts as a real unified control-plane bus instead of a thin mailbox shim. It owns a typed service directory (`DirectoryCommand`, `DirectoryResponse`, `ServiceDescriptor`, `ServiceClass`) and synchronous request/response helpers for `EchDisplay`, `EchInput`, `EchAudio`, `EchStore`, `EchShell`, `EchNotifications`, `EchClipboard`, `EchDialogs`, and `EchCapture`.
- `src/gui/client.rs` no longer drives desktop control-plane behavior through ad hoc service handles. Window lifecycle, scene commits, permission checks, file dialogs, clipboard, notifications, shell session state, capture, input polling, and store operations now resolve through the unified bus helpers. The only intentional direct handle left is the zero-copy shared-surface fast path, where the bus remains control-plane only and bulk pixel transfer stays out-of-band.
- `src/services/ech_notifications.rs`, `src/services/ech_clipboard.rs`, `src/services/ech_dialogs.rs`, and `src/services/ech_capture.rs` now query shell permissions through the same bus seam. `src/services/ech_audio.rs` exposes its command processor at `pub(crate)` so `service_ipc` can project audio requests into the same synchronous contract instead of fabricating ACK-only behavior.
- Why: the desktop client had drifted into a split-brain model where some paths used a notional service bus while most control-plane work still poked services directly. That made the “unified IPC & Service Bus” story architecturally false and prevented policy, diagnostics, and launch/runtime coordination from having one truthful entry point.
- Boundary: this unifies the desktop control-plane, not bulk data transport. Framebuffers, shared surfaces, and other hot-path payloads remain intentionally out-of-band; the bus is for discovery, request/response, lifecycle, permissions, and service orchestration.

## 2026-03-23 | pointer-bounds-auto-detect-pass | Mouse clamp now auto-syncs to the live framebuffer size at desktop init

- `src/services/ech_display.rs` now pushes the live GOP framebuffer dimensions into `crate::drivers::mouse::set_bounds(...)` during `EchDisplay::new(...)`. The pointer clamp is therefore derived from the actual display contract instead of the old baked-in `1280x800` legacy defaults inside the PS/2 mouse driver.
- `src/drivers/mouse.rs` keeps the driver-local clamp owner, but its bounds are now initialized by the display service and covered by unit tests. This closes the 1080p regression where QEMU rendered a larger desktop while the cursor still saturated at the legacy right edge.
- Why: display resolution is now runtime-variable from QEMU launch parameters, so pointer reach cannot be hardcoded. The correct ownership split is: framebuffer reports the truth, display service publishes it, mouse driver clamps against that truth.
- Verification boundary: the new tests prove `set_bounds(...)` preserves in-range coordinates and clamps oversized legacy coordinates into the new screen rect. Full `cargo test --no-run --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib` and touched-path gate should stay clean.

## 2026-03-23 | qemu-1080p-default-pass | QEMU now boots echOS with an explicit 1920x1080 virtual VGA mode by default

- `run_qemu.ps1` now accepts `-DisplayWidth` / `-DisplayHeight` and defaults them to `1920` / `1080`.
- Both ISO and UEFI launch paths inject `-vga none` plus `-device VGA,xres=<w>,yres=<h>,edid=on`, so the guest gets an explicit 1080p virtual display instead of relying on QEMU's lower default mode.
- Why: the desktop had become visually too small in the default VM window because the guest framebuffer resolution was not being forced high enough. The right fix is to set the virtual display mode at QEMU launch, not to fake larger UI scaling inside echOS.
- Verification boundary: script syntax and touched-path gate should stay clean; live visual confirmation still comes from the next GUI QEMU boot.

## 2026-03-23 | top-bar-single-rail-pass | Top bar now uses a flatter single-rail desktop language instead of grouped boxes

- `src/gfx/velvet_glove.rs` no longer presents the top bar as a row of separate framed pills. `Apps` and workspace items are now treated as text-first tabs with only a bottom accent line, the command area is the single centered capsule in the bar, status items read as inline telemetry with thin separators plus accent dots, and the clock becomes a quiet right anchor instead of another boxed tile.
- Why: the previous refinement removed some noise but still looked like a debug HUD made of adjacent boxes. The user feedback was specifically about the design shape itself, so the next truthful step was to change the language from "many chrome rectangles" to "one calm rail with a few emphasis points".
- Verification boundary: targeted top-bar layout/raster tests remain the guardrail; touched-path gate should stay clean. This changes style and hierarchy, not interaction semantics.

## 2026-03-23 | top-bar-visual-refinement-pass | Top bar now reads as one calmer system rail instead of a row of hard-edged boxes

- `src/gfx/velvet_glove.rs` now renders the top bar with a softer hierarchy. The wordmark has its own quiet left anchor, workspace pills are slimmer with underline emphasis instead of hard full-border weight, the command strip is visually centered with a compact search marker, and the right-side status capsules use low-noise rails with accent indicators instead of loud boxed chrome.
- The layout widths were tightened at the same time so the command strip has more breathing room and the status cluster no longer dominates the bar. `Apps`, workspace pills, command strip, and time capsule still keep the same interaction semantics; only the geometry and raster language changed.
- Why: the previous bar was technically readable but looked like a row of debugging boxes. The desktop had already become much cleaner than before, so the top bar became the one remaining area that still broke the product feel.
- Verification boundary: the existing top-bar raster/layout tests remain valid, full `cargo test --no-run --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib` passed, and touched-path gate stayed clean.

## 2026-03-22 | desktop-task-strip-dock-revert-pass | Bottom strip is again an app-first dock instead of a workspace panel

- `src/gfx/velvet_glove.rs` no longer treats the bottom strip as a `Spaces` control surface. The raster path in `paint_task_strip_surface(...)` now renders the centered five-slot app dock again, matching the older product direction where the bottom chrome is a launch/attention strip for applications rather than a second workspace control bar.
- Input routing for `self.shell.task_strip.window_id` was narrowed to app activation only. Right-click still opens the window context menu for a live app slot, but left-click now routes only through `task_strip_app_hit(...)`; the old task-strip workspace selection behavior is intentionally gone so the bottom strip stops competing with the top bar's workspace pills.
- Why: the user-facing desktop direction is now clear. Workspace switching already lives in the top bar, while the lower `Spaces` strip made the desktop feel cluttered and regressed the earlier app-dock affordance that was more legible and more useful.
- Verification boundary: `cargo test --no-run --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib` and touched-path gate passed after the dock raster/input revert. Runtime QEMU smoke remains noisy because the general desktop/browser boot path still has unrelated issues, but the code contract for the lower strip is back to app-dock semantics.

## 2026-03-22 | desktop-visibility-recovery-pass | Active desktop state now force-clears stale login/loading chrome

- `src/gfx/velvet_glove.rs` now runs `enforce_desktop_visibility_contract()` inside the main desktop tick both before and after command application. The recovery gate reads the live `SessionSnapshot` and, when the session power state is already `Active`, force-clears any stale login/loading shell state that still claims the desktop: it hides the lock screen, restores top bar and task strip visibility, reapplies workspace visibility for Terminal/Files/Web/Settings/Editor, refocuses the top bar, and dirties the shell for a full repaint.
- Why: the serial boot log already proved the appliance session reached `desktop-ready`, `app-basket-ready`, and `BOOTCTRL success`, while live GUI boots could still look "stuck on loading" because the lock/login surface or its visibility bookkeeping remained inconsistent with the active session state. That was a desktop visibility contract break, not a true boot halt.
- The recovery logic is intentionally fail-safe and non-destructive: it only arms when the session snapshot says `Active` and the shell should already be on the desktop (`logged_in`, `desktop_ready_published`, or pending appliance auto-login), but the shell still exposes stale lock-screen or missing top-bar/task-strip state.
- Verification boundary: pure helper tests for the recovery predicate and full `cargo test --no-run --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib` passed. This closes the stale login/loading visibility seam; browser networking and any later kernel panic remain separate issues.

## 2026-03-22 | desktop-failsafe-topbar-browser-start-pass | Top bar status clicks are temporarily notice-only and browser launch seeds a local start page

- `src/gfx/velvet_glove.rs` now treats the top-bar status and clock capsules as read-only telemetry while the overlay/power panel path is being hardened. Mouse clicks on `NET`, `CPU`, `VPN`, `AUD`, `PWR`, and the clock now emit explicit notices instead of routing into lock-screen or shell overlay chains that were surfacing as blank central panels in live QEMU.
- Why: user-visible behavior had become actively misleading. The dock path was stable, but top-bar clicks could still open a dark modal/panel with little or no visible content and, in the power case, risk an accidental lock-screen transition. Until the overlay path is repaired truthfully, the top bar is safer as visible telemetry plus non-destructive notices.
- `BrowserApp::show_start_page()` now seeds a local start page with explicit instructions and a boot-safe default address in the input field. Browser launch no longer relies on immediate network success to prove that the window path is alive.
- Verification boundary: targeted browser start-page test, browser shortcut launch test, `cargo test --no-run --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib`, and touched-path gate passed. `run_qemu.ps1 -Profile fast -Headless` still failed its existing `login-visible` marker check, so the desktop/runtime smoke remains only partially revalidated in automation.

## 2026-03-21 | shell-overlay-buffer-present-pass | Shell chrome now uses the same direct buffer present path as browser windows

- `src/gfx/velvet_glove.rs` no longer routes shell layer windows through `commit_scene(raster_surface_scene(...))`. `commit_shell_surface(...)` now uses `DesktopClient::present(...)`, and the task strip moved from `build_task_strip_scene(...)` onto `paint_task_strip_surface(...)` so desktop wallpaper, top bar, dock, launcher, notifications, quick settings, command palette, overview, dialog, context menu, and switcher all share one direct buffer-present seam.
- The regression boundary was concrete: browser windows were visibly rendering text and controls in live QEMU through `present(...)`, while shell overlays could surface panel geometry without reliable text/content under the layer-window scene compositor route. Unifying shell chrome onto the browser-proven buffer path removes that split-brain rendering contract.
- Boundary: this closes the rendering path mismatch, not browser networking. If the browser still shows `ConnectionFailed`, that remaining fault is in the network/request chain rather than shell UI presentation.

## 2026-03-21 | shell-overlay-raster-browser-bootstrap-pass | Interactive shell overlays now commit through raster surfaces and browser requests bootstrap runtime networking first

- `src/gfx/velvet_glove.rs` now commits the user-facing shell overlays that were visually blank in live QEMU through the already-proven raster path instead of the scene-text path: launcher, notification center, quick settings, command palette, workspace overview, dialog broker, context menu, and app switcher all use `paint_*_surface(...)` plus `commit_shell_surface(...)`. This deliberately trades animated scene richness for truthful visible content on the live compositor path.
- The regression boundary was concrete: top-bar and desktop actions did open the right windows, but the scene overlay route produced mostly empty chrome/panel geometry in live GUI boots. Raster surfaces already rendered text correctly for desktop and top bar, so the safest repair was to unify overlays onto the same commit seam.
- `BrowserApp::open_current_url()` and `download_current_url()` now call `crate::net::smoltcp_driver::ensure_runtime_network()` before using `HttpClient`. Browser launch no longer assumes the network stack is pre-bootstrapped by unrelated callers. The default browser homepage seed was also changed to `http://example.com/` so the first visible success path does not depend on the TLS path closing first.
- Boundary: this does not prove full browser network parity or final overlay polish. It closes two user-visible lies: "panel opened but looked empty" and "browser window launched but did not even attempt a boot-safe network path".

## 2026-03-21 | browser-pe-diagnostics-pass | Browser-class PE launch now publishes actionable missing-import telemetry instead of a generic spawn failure

- `src/pe_loader.rs` now exposes immutable `PeLaunchDiagnostics` with `PeImportFailure` entries. The loader can preflight a PE payload, resolve the import graph against the Win32/ABI bridge, and retain the exact unresolved `dll!symbol` list instead of collapsing everything into `ImportNotFound` without context.
- `src/runtime.rs` now runs that PE preflight before `spawn_process_task_from_payload(...)`. Browser-class external images therefore fail early with a truthful label such as `... missing import browserhelper.dll!CreateSandboxBroker (+N more)` instead of the old opaque `PE runtime spawn failed`.
- Contract boundary: this does not close browser compatibility. It closes the diagnostic lie. When Firefox/Chromium/CEF launch breaks in the import graph, desktop notices now point to the first unresolved seam and the remaining fan-out count; child-process graphs, sandbox helpers after entry transfer, and graphics/runtime behavior remain later slices.

## 2026-03-21 | browser-binary-alias-pass | Desktop launcher now treats Firefox/Chromium/CEF as first-class Win32 browser targets

- `src/gui/launch_pipeline.rs` widened `AppResolution` and `PackageRecord` so alias resolution can represent three truthful states instead of only "built-in vs raw path": built-in native apps, resolved external image paths, and known external packages whose candidate binaries are not currently installed. The new `resolve_launch_query_with_probe(...)` seam keeps package aliases from pretending a browser exists when no `.exe` is present.
- `src/runtime.rs` now exposes `RuntimePackageRegistry::resolve_with_probe(...)`, keeping the canonical launch/present pipeline intact while allowing desktop surfaces to supply a real filesystem existence probe.
- `src/gfx/velvet_glove.rs` gained a first-class desktop launch registry for serious browser binaries: `firefox`, `chromium`/`chrome`, and `cef`/`cefclient` now resolve through named PE+Win32+ShellOwned descriptors instead of requiring users to type raw paths. The desktop command palette and terminal shortcuts (`open firefox`, `open chromium`, `open chrome`, `open cef`) will launch the first discovered installed candidate, or emit a truthful "searched these paths" notice when none are present.
- The Files app now detects `.exe` / `.elf` payloads through the same canonical external-image seam, so downloaded executables stop falling back to the editor path and instead route into `LaunchExternal` with the correct loader/ABI contract.
- Boundary: this does not mean Firefox/Chromium now run end-to-end. It closes the first launch/discovery lie by making browser binaries first-class launcher targets and by making missing-install state explicit. Child-process graphs, DLL breadth, sandbox, and browser-class graphics behavior remain the next compatibility slices.

## 2026-03-21 | browser-compat-program-plan | Windows browser binary compatibility will be pursued before any native web-engine rewrite

- Decision: echOS browser strategy is now explicitly split into two layers. Layer 1 is the existing native GUI downloader/browser shell (`src/gfx/velvet_glove.rs`) for immediate URL preview, download, and package/bootstrap flows. Layer 2 is a Windows-binary browser compatibility program focused on running a heavyweight existing browser under the Win32/PE runtime instead of attempting a fresh native Chromium/Gecko-class engine.
- Rationale: The user goal is not "invent a browser engine", it is "gain a serious browser inside echOS while simultaneously increasing the OS' ability to install and run existing Windows applications". A browser such as Firefox or a Chromium/CEF-family app is therefore valuable as a stress corpus for process trees, DLL resolution, user32/gdi32 behavior, HTTP/TLS, downloads, shell integration, timers, GPU present flow, and sandbox/child-process contracts.
- Chosen order of attack:
  1. `BROW-01`: desktop/browser UX remains good enough to fetch payloads now via the native shell browser, but no new engine work is prioritized ahead of compatibility.
  2. `BROW-02`: establish browser-binary launch as the main milestone. First target class is Win32 browser binaries or Chromium-embedded apps rather than source-porting Firefox/Chromium.
  3. `BROW-03`: expand runtime compatibility around the browser, specifically child-process creation, DLL/load graph truthfulness, window/timer/message-loop fidelity, filesystem download/rename/open flows, proxy/TLS/session behavior, and graphics/swapchain/present semantics.
  4. `BROW-04`: once a serious browser binary is stably launchable, reuse that widened compatibility surface for other heavyweight Windows apps instead of treating browser work as a one-off.
- Preferred executable targets:
  - Phase A: smaller Chromium-embedded or browser-adjacent Windows binaries when they offer a narrower dependency graph.
  - Phase B: Firefox-class browser launch as the real compatibility proving ground.
  - Phase C: only after that, revisit whether a deeper native web engine (for example Servo or a Chromium embed host) is still strategically necessary.
- Explicit non-goal for now: do not source-port Chromium or Firefox into echOS as a first move. That path is too large, shifts effort into upstream build/runtime ecology, and does less for Win32 application compatibility than executing existing binaries directly.
- Evidence boundary: this is a durable attack plan, not a closure claim. It should stay in memory until converted into named TODO items and mechanical corpora.

## 2026-03-21 | browser-crate-seam-pass | GUI browser now uses a vendored-ready HTML tokenizer seam instead of ad hoc tag scraping

- `Cargo.toml` now pulls `htmlparser 0.2.1` with `default-features = false`. The crate was selected because it is small, token-centric, and narrow enough to adopt without dragging a web-engine runtime into echOS' GUI shell.
- Mutation scope is intentionally narrow: `src/gfx/velvet_glove.rs` uses `htmlparser` only for browser preview/link discovery. Networking, HTTP transport, file writes, download persistence, and shell/window ownership stay on echOS-local `HttpClient` and `DesktopClient` contracts.
- Exposed behavior boundary: the browser is a pragmatic downloader/browser shell, not a full layout or JavaScript engine. HTML responses are summarized into preview lines plus absolute link extraction, and binary responses are directed into `/downloads/...` through the same GUI app window.
- Verification coverage: browser URL normalization, HTML title/link extraction, download target derivation, launch-pipeline tests, full `cargo test --no-run --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib`, touched-path gate, and headless QEMU smoke.

## 2026-03-21 | whole-os-runtime-spine-pass | Canonical launch/present pipeline now crosses desktop, services, scheduler, PE, and Win32 seams

- `src/runtime.rs` now owns the whole-system runtime spine instead of leaving the canonical contract trapped inside the desktop shell. The repository has explicit names for runtime ownership (`RuntimeHandle`, `RuntimeCoordinator`) and window-session ownership (`WindowSessionHandle`, `WindowSessionSpec`) plus a generic `RuntimePackageRegistry`.
- `src/gui/launch_pipeline.rs` widened the launch contract with `LaunchSource::ServiceInit`, `AppPresentation::Headless`, `RuntimeBootstrap::NativeHeadless`, and `UnifiedEventLoopContract::HeadlessService`. This lets service tasks live on the same launch/bootstrap graph as windowed native apps and shell-owned PE/ELF images instead of bypassing the model entirely.
- `src/services/mod.rs` no longer spawns services directly through raw `scheduler::spawn_with_priority(...)`. Service boot now registers through `runtime::spawn_service_runtime(...)`, so `EchDisplay`, `EchInput`, `EchAudio`, capture, clipboard, dialogs, notifications, shell, and store tasks all publish canonical runtime handles.
- `src/task/scheduler.rs` gained `spawn_user_image_task(...)`, which builds a real Ring-3 task from an ELF image without hijacking the current shell/compositor task. `src/gfx/velvet_glove.rs` therefore no longer uses `enter_user_elf_from_image(...)` for desktop-launched ELF binaries; it spawns them as proper scheduled tasks via the runtime spine.
- `src/pe_loader.rs` gained a scheduled PE task trampoline and `spawn_process_task_from_payload(...)`, so shell-owned PE images no longer stop at staging/reporting; they now enter the same runtime handle registry as ELF and service launches. `src/win32.rs` mirrors that with `spawn_bound_process_task(...)`, removing another raw task-spawn bypass from `CreateProcessA`.
- `src/gui/client.rs` now attaches and releases canonical window-session handles on create/destroy. That means `Window Object + IPC Endpoints` are no longer only an implied pairing of display service state and ad hoc client bookkeeping.
- Evidence boundary: targeted `gui::launch_pipeline::tests::*`, new `runtime::tests::*`, the desktop special-shortcut exact corpus, full `cargo test --no-run --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib`, touched-path gate, and headless QEMU smoke all passed.

## 2026-03-21 | canonical-launch-intent-pass | Native desktop launches now flow through one named LaunchIntent contract instead of ad hoc activate-app calls

- `src/gui/launch_pipeline.rs` introduces the canonical launch contract for desktop surfaces: `LaunchSource`, `LoaderDispatch`, `AbiPersonality`, `CapabilityProfile`, `AppDescriptor`, `ExecutionContext`, and `LaunchIntent`.
- The contract is now widened into explicit runtime ownership objects too: `ProcessContract`, `RuntimeBootstrap`, `WindowEndpointContract`, `UnifiedEventLoopContract`, and `LaunchSession`. Desktop code no longer has to infer bootstrap shape ad hoc from scattered enums.
- `src/gfx/velvet_glove.rs` now routes desktop shortcut activation, command-palette app launch, shell shortcut launch, task-strip launch, launcher launch, notification launch, file-association launch, context-menu focus, fault auto-restore, and editor document-open through `dispatch_launch_intent(...)`. The policy check therefore lives in one place instead of being bypassed by multiple direct `activate_app(...)` branches or `SessionCommand::Activate`.
- Scope widened in the same pass: `src/gui/launch_pipeline.rs` now resolves external image paths into first-class descriptors (`.exe` -> `PE + Win32 Bridge + ShellOwned`, `.elf/.bin` -> `ELF + POSIX Bridge + ShellOwned`), and `src/gfx/velvet_glove.rs` command-palette query execution can dispatch those external images through the same policy gate before handing off to the existing runtime bridges.
- Desktop non-window actions are also first-class descriptors now: `Web` and `Recycle Bin` publish `Native + SpecialAction` contracts instead of bypassing the launcher as ad hoc notices.
- Built-in package/app discovery is no longer path-only: the desktop now resolves registry-backed aliases such as `terminal`, `files`, `settings`, `editor`, `web`, and `recycle bin` through the same launch resolution seam used by path-based PE/ELF images.
- Event ingestion is now formalized as one `UnifiedEventLoopBatch` in `src/gfx/velvet_glove.rs`, so shell/app loops are coordinated by an explicit orchestration object before command application and present.
- Evidence: new unit tests in `src/gui/launch_pipeline.rs` pin descriptor/permission export semantics, full `cargo test --no-run --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib` stays green, and `echos_gate` is clean for the touched pipeline files.

## 2026-03-21 | desktop-topbar-runtime-status-pass | Top bar now uses an apps/workspace/search/status/clock contract instead of anonymous chrome

- `src/gfx/velvet_glove.rs` no longer treats the upper chrome as empty pill geometry. The bar is now laid out as five explicit clusters: `echOS`, an `Apps` pill, named workspace pills, a centered `Super+Space  Search / Run / Open` strip, and a right-side runtime status rail.
- The right-side status rail now carries truthful live summaries for `NET`, `CPU`, `VPN`, `AUD`, and `PWR`, with the clock/date isolated as its own far-right capsule. Runtime strings come from existing stack owners instead of synthetic labels: `smoltcp_driver` for link state, scheduler load for CPU, WireGuard runtime status for VPN, audio mixer or jail controller state for audio, and RTC cached time for the clock.
- Input routing now matches the visual contract. `Apps` opens the command palette launcher surface, workspace pills switch workspaces, the command strip opens search/run, `NET`/`VPN`/`AUD` open quick settings, `CPU` surfaces a quick runtime notice, `PWR` toggles the power state, and the clock pill announces the current wall clock. This keeps the bar operational without reintroducing tutorial/dashboard cards into the clean desktop.

## 2026-03-21 | desktop-topbar-raster-commit-pass | Boot top bar now commits through the raster shell-surface path so labels survive the live QEMU compositor path

- `src/gfx/velvet_glove.rs` no longer commits the top bar through the retained scene path during the boot desktop invalidation pass. The live top bar now goes through `commit_shell_surface(...)` using `paint_top_bar_surface(...)`, which is the same text raster path already proven visible in Terminal, Files, and Settings surfaces.
- Why: QEMU was still showing the new top-bar geometry as anonymous dark pills even after the scene layout and labels were updated. The pills proved the geometry branch was active, but the missing labels proved the live boot path was dropping text raster somewhere between scene assembly and presentation. The raster shell-surface path removes that ambiguity.
- Validation boundary: a new unit test proves the raster top bar surface contains non-background glyph pixels in both the left title zone and the center command-strip zone, so regressions back to label-free pills are mechanically visible.

## 2026-03-21 | desktop-topbar-functional-cluster-pass | Top bar now spends its chrome budget on workspace, search, and system status instead of empty placeholder boxes

- `src/gfx/velvet_glove.rs` top-bar layout now treats the upper chrome as three functional clusters: named workspace pills on the left (`Prime`, `Build`, `Observe`, `Docs` and the higher workspace families when the active group rolls), a wider command palette strip in the center, and compact `NET` / `AUD` / `PWR` plus `Quick`, `12:00`, and `Lock` controls on the right.
- The raster and scene render paths now share the same geometry and labels, so QEMU no longer shows anonymous empty top-bar boxes while the retained scene path paints richer content elsewhere.
- The command strip intentionally reads as a launcher contract rather than a tutorial banner: `Super+Space  Search / Run / Open`. This keeps the bar informative without reintroducing dashboard prose into the clean desktop.

## 2026-03-21 | desktop-free-shortcut-column-pass | Clean boot desktop now uses free-standing shortcut icons instead of tutorial panels

- `src/gui/shell.rs` no longer renders `Desktop Ready`, `System Pulse`, `Session Status`, `Desktop`, or `Quick Start` cards in clean-desktop mode. The wallpaper now carries a classic desktop shortcut column: icon first, label directly below, no enclosing dashboard box.
- The intent is explicitly Windows-like rather than dashboard-like: users should read the first screen as a desktop with launch points, not as a help/tutorial surface.
- `src/gui/icon_pack.rs` keeps the new `Browser` and `Recycle` glyphs, so the wallpaper vocabulary now covers the expected desktop staples without inventing extra chrome.

## 2026-03-21 | desktop-clean-state-dashboard-gate | Ambient dashboard cards now render only on a truly clean desktop

- `src/services/ech_display.rs` now queries the shell session snapshot before compositing the root desktop. The background renderer only enables the richer dashboard cards when the shell reports `boot_clean_desktop`, `!overview_active`, and `ShellState::DesktopReady`.
- `src/gui/shell.rs` now treats the ambient dashboard as an explicit mode instead of always-on chrome. The wallpaper gradient remains the universal base layer, but the `Desktop Ready`, `System Pulse`, and related cards are suppressed once real app windows are active.
