## 2026-03-09 | win32-driver-security | Native ABI bridge + onboarding matrix + anti-cheat parity contract
- Decision: Extended PE execution contract in `src/pe_loader.rs` with explicit staged APIs (`resolve_imports`, `init_tls`, `spawn_process`, `transfer_entry`, `spawn_process_from_payload`), wired `src/win32.rs` resolver to `ntdll` and `ws2_32` through `src/win32_abi.rs`, introduced `ntdll` syscall thunks and `ws2_32` socket ABI bridge, added driver onboarding profiles and compatibility matrix APIs in `src/drivers/dispatcher.rs` with mandatory eLS source-compat and IronShim manifest policy flags, added anti-cheat parity module `src/security/anti_cheat.rs`, and surfaced parity+driver matrix snapshots from `src/ecosystem.rs`.
- Rationale: The main compatibility plan required public contracts that tie native Windows execution, graphics/game path prerequisites, and broad Linux driver onboarding to concrete kernel surfaces instead of roadmap-only intent.
- Tradeoffs: ABI bridge currently covers the mandatory nucleus (`ntdll` and core `ws2_32` sockets) rather than full WinSock/NT breadth; PE process spawn still uses in-kernel transfer helper semantics and depends on existing image mapping flow.
- Evidence: `cargo check --quiet` and `.\.qoder\skills\echos-architect\scripts\echos_gate.ps1 -Paths src/pe_loader.rs,src/win32.rs,src/win32_abi.rs,src/drivers/dispatcher.rs,src/ecosystem.rs,src/security/mod.rs,src/security/anti_cheat.rs` planned as post-patch gate for this slice.
- Supersedes: The previous state where PE lifecycle stages were implicit, resolver coverage omitted `ntdll/ws2_32`, onboarding matrix was not explicit, and anti-cheat parity had no dedicated kernel contract module.
- Next validation: Run PE import/TLS lifecycle tests and ws2 loopback compatibility checks, then extend NT thunk coverage and anti-cheat attestation signals against provider-facing parity checklists.

## 2026-03-10 | input | Mouse micro-delta preservation in EchInput routing
- Decision: Updated `src/services/ech_input.rs` mouse translation to emit `PointerMove` using filtered delta when available, but fallback to raw delta when filter quantizes motion to zero while absolute pointer position changed.
- Rationale: Simics/PS2 streams can produce frequent `Â±1` deltas; deadzone quantization previously dropped those events, making the shell cursor appear frozen despite updated hardware coordinates.
- Tradeoffs: Very small jitter can now pass through when filter output is zero, but this is bounded to real position changes only and keeps pointer responsiveness deterministic.
- Evidence: `.\.qoder\skills\echos-architect\scripts\echos_gate.ps1 -Paths src/services/ech_input.rs`.
- Supersedes: The prior input behavior where zeroed filtered deltas suppressed pointer moves entirely.
- Next validation: Re-run Simics interactive pointer movement test and confirm continuous cursor motion under low-amplitude PS/2 deltas.

## 2026-03-10 | display-hotpath | Scheduler telemetry contract and race-proof test matrix
- Decision: Extended `FrameIntent` with `enqueue_timestamp_ns` and upgraded `src/services/display_atomic.rs` with hot-path telemetry accounting (`produced`, `dropped`, `presented`, `direct_scanout`, commit/total latency sums and maxima, missed-vblank total), added `HotPathMetrics` snapshot API on both `FrameScheduler` and `AtomicPresenter`, exposed metrics through `DisplayCommand::QueryPresentMetrics` + `DisplayResponse::PresentMetrics` in `src/services/ech_display.rs`, and added a client accessor in `src/gui/client.rs`.
- Rationale: The plan acceptance gates (`rho_drop`, `eta_scanout`, `L_commit`, `L_total`) needed runtime-observable counters and a query surface instead of implicit internal state.
- Tradeoffs: Unit tests for the new scheduler state-machine paths are implemented in `display_atomic.rs` using local DRM test devices, but repository-wide `cargo test` remains blocked by existing no-std test-harness issues unrelated to this slice.
- Evidence: `cargo check --quiet` passes after the telemetry + API wiring; `cargo test --quiet display_atomic::tests -- --test-threads=1` still fails globally due pre-existing crate-wide `can't find crate for test` and unrelated legacy test errors.
- Supersedes: The prior display-hotpath state where queue/flip behavior existed but quantitative acceptance metrics had no externally queryable contract.
- Next validation: Run scheduler tests once the repository test harness is fixed, then capture 60/120/240Hz stress traces and assert `rho_drop < 0.5%` under steady-state workloads.

## 2026-03-10 | host-build | Host-safe interrupt ABI split for Windows codegen
- Decision: Split interrupt and boot-facing ABI surfaces into host-safe logic and bare-metal wrappers by gating `abi_x86_interrupt`, IDT handler registration, Limine/global-asm entry paths, and Windows host entry/runtime shims across `src/lib.rs`, `src/platform/interrupt_abi.rs`, `src/cpu/interrupts.rs`, `src/interrupts/mod.rs`, and `src/main.rs`; Windows host builds now verify architecture contracts without codegen of `extern "x86-interrupt"` handlers.
- Rationale: `rustc 1.93.0-nightly (2025-11-18)` on Windows host was tripping `offset is not a multiple of 16` when backend-lowering bare-metal interrupt ABI paths, so the durable fix was to remove those paths from host codegen rather than pinning the toolchain.
- Tradeoffs: Host binaries are compile-time inert stubs for boot/interrupt behavior and are not intended to emulate silicon semantics; the real ISR ABI remains active only on `target_os = "none"` and `target_os = "uefi"`.
- Evidence: `cargo build --lib --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc -q`, `cargo build --lib --target x86_64-pc-windows-gnu -q`, and `cargo build --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc -q` pass after the split.
- Supersedes: The previous configuration where Windows-host codegen still instantiated bare-metal interrupt wrappers and COFF-incompatible boot entry surfaces.
- Next validation: Keep host builds in CI as codegen gates while expanding host-safe no-op coverage for other privileged asm surfaces if future compiler regressions surface.

## 2026-03-10 | win32-graphics-driver-security | Typed NT handles + DXGI bridge + IronShim parity integration
- Decision: Reworked `src/win32_abi.rs` around a typed global handle table (`File`, `Process`, `Thread`, `Socket`, `Waitable`) with real `NtCreateFile`/`NtReadFile`/`NtCreateProcess`/`NtWaitForSingleObject`/`NtClose` behavior, upgraded `src/pe_loader.rs` with richer process descriptors plus an orchestrated PE lifecycle report, replaced `ecosystem` DXVK string mapping with a `DxgiPresentBridge` that primes `gpu3d` shader/pipeline/fence caches and submits `FrameIntent` into `ech_display`, enforced IronShim manifest acceptance in `src/drivers/dispatcher.rs`, and extended `src/security/anti_cheat.rs` + `src/debug/kgdb.rs` with signed-driver policy enforcement, runtime violation emission, debug-attach deny, telemetry-gap reporting, and attestation reason codes.
- Rationale: The remaining ana plan slice needed real contracts between PE loading, Win32 execution, D3D11/DXGI present pacing, driver onboarding, and anti-cheat parity instead of parallel placeholder policy layers.
- Tradeoffs: DXGI translation is present-first and D3D11-class rather than full DX12 parity; driver signature acceptance is currently policy/enforcement based rather than cryptographic vendor PKI verification; attestation reason codes model kernel parity signals and runtime deltas without yet integrating third-party provider SDKs.
- Evidence: `cargo check -q` and Windows host library builds pass after wiring the new contracts; dispatcher activation now records manifest summaries and parity violations at acceptance time.
- Supersedes: The earlier state where NT handles were hash/success stubs, `ecosystem` held a DXVK string map instead of a live bridge, driver onboarding did not gate activation through IronShim manifests, and anti-cheat parity exposed only snapshots without reason-coded attestation.
- Next validation: Add focused host-run unit tests for handle table/process lifecycle/DXGI bridge/attestation once the repository test harness cleanup is complete, then stress a real RTX baseline with PE launch + present pacing + driver scan onboarding traces.

## 2026-03-10 | display | GOP fallback required when atomic scanout is unavailable
- Decision: Updated `src/services/ech_display.rs` so `present()` only takes the atomic-present fast path when at least one native GPU device is registered; on UEFI/Simics GOP-only boots it now bypasses synthetic atomic completion and always executes the framebuffer composition fallback. `service_present_queue()` now returns `Ack` immediately when no native GPU device exists.
- Rationale: Desktop bootstrap reached `Velvet Glove` readiness, but the screen remained on splash because the atomic presenter reported synthetic completion without any real scanout target, which skipped the only path that writes composed pixels into the GOP framebuffer.
- Tradeoffs: GOP-only environments lose atomic-present telemetry progression during active composition, but they regain correct visibility; true KMS/plane accounting remains intact on systems with native GPU registration.
- Evidence: `cargo check --target x86_64-unknown-uefi --features simics -q` passes; `.\.qoder\skills\echos-architect\scripts\echos_gate.ps1 -Paths src/services/ech_display.rs` reports warning-only audit notes.
- Supersedes: The previous presenter behavior where host/sim synthetic VBLANK completion could satisfy `present()` before any framebuffer fallback draw occurred.
- Next validation: Reboot in Simics and confirm the boot splash is replaced by the login/desktop shell immediately after `[DESKTOP] session bootstrap step=ready`, then re-check pointer motion on the visible shell surface.

## 2026-03-10 | task-abi | Unified address-wait core for rseq, futex_waitv, WaitOnAddress, and NT waitables
- Decision: Added per-task `RseqState` in `src/task/task.rs` plus `src/task/rseq.rs` registration/snapshot logic; introduced a shared address-wait manager in `src/task/futex.rs` that parks boxed tasks, wakes by address, tracks timeout/signaled outcomes, and backs `sys_futex`, `sys_futex_waitv`, `wait_on_address`, `wake_by_address_single`, and `wake_by_address_all`; routed `src/posix.rs` futex/rseq syscalls to the `task` implementations; and rewired `src/win32_abi.rs` + `src/win32.rs` so `NtWaitForSingleObject`, `NtCreateEvent`/`NtSetEvent`/`NtResetEvent`, `WaitOnAddress`, and kernel32 event waits share the same core wait path. A Windows-visible I/O Ring handle wrapper was also added over the existing `io_uring` core for create/register/submit/close flow.
- Rationale: The repo had three parallel wait semantics (`task::futex`, `posix` local futex list, and Win32 sync objects) with mismatched wake behavior and no common latest contract for Tier-1 ABI parity.
- Tradeoffs: `futex_waitv` currently returns the first matching waited address index after a shared wake record rather than carrying richer per-vector kernel metadata, and the Windows I/O Ring wrapper currently covers create/register/submit lifecycle without a full BuildRead/BuildWrite SQE authoring surface.
- Evidence: `cargo check -q`; `cargo build --lib --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc -q`.
- Supersedes: The prior state where futex wait used sleep-based pseudo-blocking, `posix.rs` carried its own waiter table, `WaitForSingleObject` returned unconditional success, and Win32 address waits/I/O Ring had no shared kernel contract.
- Next validation: Add focused runtime tests for `rseq` migration refresh, `futex_waitv` multi-address wake ordering, NT event auto-reset/manual-reset behavior, and the Windows I/O Ring registered-resource path under Simics/QEMU userland smoke.

## 2026-03-10 | memory-scheduler-policy | DAMON-guided reclaim and PSI-fed scheduler pressure control
- Decision: Added `src/memory/damon.rs` as a DAMON-style page-temperature sampler, wired it into `src/memory/mod.rs` page-access/refault/eviction paths, and blended DAMON victim hints with existing MGLRU reclaim selection so hot pages are preserved outside severe pressure while cold pages are reclaimed earlier. In parallel, `src/task/scheduler.rs` now derives a scheduler pressure class from PSI averages and uses it to bias time-slice sizing, locality-sensitive steal scoring, and GhOSt event publication on task lifecycle transitions; `src/task/ghost.rs` now provides a real lock-free event ring plus agent registry/watchdog snapshot instead of comment-only API promises.
- Rationale: The repo already had MGLRU, PSI, and GhOSt skeletons, but reclaim and scheduling decisions still lacked a shared policy signal, which left Tier-1 latency and memory-pressure behavior under-specified.
- Tradeoffs: DAMON remains a low-overhead hint engine rather than a full region-merging Linux clone, and scheduler pressure control still sits on the existing work-stealing scheduler rather than replacing it with full EEVDF or delegated sched-ext policy.
- Evidence: `cargo check -q` and `.\.qoder\skills\echos-architect\scripts\echos_gate.ps1 -Paths src\memory\damon.rs,src\memory\mod.rs,src\task\scheduler.rs,src\task\ghost.rs` after wiring.
- Supersedes: The prior state where reclaim considered only LRU/MGLRU age without a direct access sampler, scheduler slices ignored PSI pressure, and `task::ghost` exposed only design commentary without a kernel event queue implementation.
- Next validation: Stress reclaim under synthetic refault churn, verify hot-page preservation outside severe PSI thresholds, and smoke Ghost event delivery plus scheduler behavior in Simics/QEMU runs with mixed foreground/background loads.

## 2026-03-10 | win32-fence-lifecycle | Queued I/O Ring file ops and monotonic GPU fence values
- Decision: Extended `src/win32_abi.rs` and `src/win32.rs` so Windows I/O Ring handles now keep registered file/buffer tables, queued read/write file operations, and explicit completion draining through `BuildIoRingReadFile`, `BuildIoRingWriteFile`, and `PopIoRingCompletion` in addition to create/register/submit. In parallel, `src/gpu3d.rs` now models fences as monotonic value timelines with target values, snapshots, and bounded signal logs, and `src/ecosystem.rs` drives present fences by target-present-id before submission and by presented-frame-id on completion.
- Rationale: The previous I/O Ring surface could only register resources and call submit, which left no end-to-end operation lifecycle for Windows-native callers; boolean fence state also could not express DXGI-style frame-progress contracts or provide a stable present timeline.
- Tradeoffs: File writes currently update the live Win32 file-handle buffer rather than persisting back into every VFS backend, and the new native-fence contract is driven by present completion IDs instead of a vendor-specific hardware fence register block.
- Evidence: `cargo check -q`, `cargo build --lib --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc -q`, and `cargo build --lib --target x86_64-pc-windows-gnu -q`.
- Supersedes: The prior state where I/O Ring stopped at resource registration and submit, and where GPU fences only exposed a boolean signaled bit.
- Next validation: Add focused runtime smoke for queued I/O Ring read/write completions and verify fence-value monotonicity under repeated DXGI present bursts in Simics/QEMU.

## 2026-03-10 | drm-shared-sync | DMA-BUF export bookkeeping and reservation-backed flip completion
- Decision: Extended `src/drivers/drm.rs` with `DmaBufExport`, `DmaReservationSnapshot`, and `DmaFenceState`, added PRIME-style export/import bookkeeping plus per-GEM reservation state, and made `commit_transaction()` reserve every selected plane buffer while `report_flip_complete()` completes those reservations and signals handle-specific native fence timelines through `gpu3d`.
- Rationale: The graphics hot path already had atomic KMS and fence timelines, but there was no object-level ownership/synchronization contract connecting selected scanout buffers to commit/flip progress.
- Tradeoffs: Buffer sharing is still device-local metadata rather than a full cross-process `dma-resv` graph with timeline imports, and reservation tracking follows selected plane handles instead of vendor-specific fence memory blocks.
- Evidence: `cargo check -q`; the new focused DRM reservation test compiles into the crate but runtime execution remains blocked by the repository-wide `can't find crate for test` harness failure.
- Supersedes: The prior state where GEM objects only carried an optional dma-buf fd and had no reservation/fence progress coupled to atomic present.
- Next validation: Run the reservation test once the repo test harness is repaired, then inspect Simics/QEMU present bursts to verify exported dma-buf handles and reservation snapshots advance monotonically across commits.

## 2026-03-10 | host-runtime-tier1 | Host allocator split, delegated policy smoke, and batched SQPOLL validation
- Decision: Routed non-`target_os = "none"` builds through a dedicated host verification path by exporting `std` in `src/lib.rs`, replacing the global allocator with a host `System`-backed wrapper in `src/allocator/mod.rs`, deriving a host-local image span in `src/memory/frame_allocator.rs`, and backing host `dma_alloc()` with aligned `std::alloc` memory in `src/memory/mod.rs`. On top of that, `src/net/io_uring.rs` now batches SQPOLL draining at `submit_sqes()` granularity instead of per-SQE auto-drain, and `tests/tier1_runtime_smoke.rs` exercises GhOSt/Valkyrie delegated policy, IOMMU PASID/SVA/GPUVA bookkeeping, DRM cross-process reservation propagation, and io_uring batched completion as a harness-free host binary.
- Rationale: The remaining Tier-1 slices needed runtime evidence before the repository test harness was repaired, but host runs were crashing on the kernel TLSF allocator and bare-metal DMA/linker assumptions long before the new logic could execute.
- Tradeoffs: Host verification builds still validate architecture contracts rather than silicon behavior; host DMA backing uses aligned process memory rather than physical frames, and the smoke binary proves control/data-flow contracts without touching real MMIO queue doorbells.
- Evidence: `cargo run --bin tier1_runtime_smoke --features host_smoke --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc -q` prints `tier1_runtime_smoke: OK`; `cargo check -q` passes; `.\.qoder\skills\echos-architect\scripts\echos_gate.ps1 -Paths src\lib.rs,src\allocator\mod.rs,src\memory\frame_allocator.rs,src\memory\mod.rs,src\net\io_uring.rs,tests\tier1_runtime_smoke.rs,src\task\ghost.rs,src\task\scheduler.rs,src\drivers\iommu.rs,src\drivers\drm.rs,src\valkyrie_virt.rs,src\drivers\mod.rs,src\net\zero_copy.rs,Cargo.toml` reports warning-only host-allocator and pre-existing atomic/alignment audits.
- Supersedes: The previous state where host smoke linked against bare-metal linker symbols, used an uninitialized TLSF allocator, failed DMA-backed ring allocation on host, and split SQPOLL completions into single-SQE batches.
- Next validation: Add the host smoke binary to CI as a required codegen/runtime gate, then follow with QEMU/Simics traces for the same delegated-policy and shared-buffer paths once the repo-wide test harness is repaired.

## 2026-03-10 | iommu-dmaresv-nvme | PRI replay queue, usage-aware dma-resv propagation, and host-verifiable NVMe SQ/CQ flow
- Decision: Extended `src/drivers/iommu.rs` with queued PRI page requests, replay results, SVA generation tracking, and ATS/IOTLB invalidation logging so PASID/SVA/GPUVA state now supports batched `queue_page_request -> process_page_requests -> invalidate_gpuva_range` flow instead of bare counters. Upgraded `src/drivers/drm.rs` so reservation edges carry usage semantics (`Read`, `Write`, `Kernel`) and commit/flip propagation walks the reachable reservation graph rather than a single producer-consumer hop. Reworked `src/drivers/nvme.rs` async path around explicit SQE writes, MMIO doorbell publication, CQ phase draining, and CID-matched completion retirement, plus a host verification constructor used by `tests/tier1_runtime_smoke.rs`.
- Rationale: The remaining Tier-1 delta needed a real replay/graph/queue engine contract behind the existing bookkeeping so host smoke could exercise page-request replay, cross-process fence propagation, and doorbell-driven completion flow without waiting on the broken unit-test harness.
- Tradeoffs: The IOMMU replay engine is still controller-generic rather than vendor-register-specific PRQ hardware microcode, dma-resv propagation models Linux-grade graph ownership/usage but not full `ww_mutex` wound-wait locking, and the NVMe host smoke uses injected CQEs over raw verification buffers instead of a live SSD controller.
- Evidence: `cargo check -q`; `cargo run --bin tier1_runtime_smoke --features host_smoke --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc -q` prints `tier1_runtime_smoke: OK`.
- Supersedes: The earlier state where page-request replay was only a replay counter, dma-buf propagation stopped at one edge without usage semantics, and async NVMe completion was synthesized from pending counters rather than SQ/CQ state.
- Next validation: Push the same flows through QEMU/Simics device models, then replace controller-generic replay/invalidation with vendor-exact PRQ/ATS register programming on the first real Tier-1 baseline.

## 2026-03-10 | controller-exact-prq-ww-doorbells | Intel VT-d PRQ programming, ww-style dma-resv acquisition, and vendor-family doorbells
- Decision: Extended `src/drivers/iommu.rs` with controller-programmed Intel VT-d page-request queue registers (`PQH/PQT/PQA/PRS/PECTL`) and AMD-Vi PPR-log register programming (`PPR_LOG_A/B`, head/tail, auto-response control), plus host-verifiable raw queue allocation, request injection, and queue snapshots. In parallel, `src/drivers/drm.rs` now routes reservation updates through a wound-wait style acquisition context with per-object ownership stamps before commit/flip propagation walks the reachable dma-resv graph. For vendor-family MMIO hot paths, `src/drivers/nic_native.rs` gained an Intel 8254x profile that drives real `TDT/RDT/IMS` doorbells, and `src/drivers/virtio_gpu.rs` now exposes an exact VirtIO GPU notify-register snapshot derived from `queue_notify_off * notify_off_multiplier`.
- Rationale: The previous slice had graph semantics and replay bookkeeping, but not controller-visible PRQ/PPR programming, not a deadlock-avoidance contract resembling Linux `ww_mutex` around reservation batches, and not explicit vendor-family doorbell/register surfaces for NIC/GPU hot paths.
- Tradeoffs: The host smoke path validates exact register programming and queue-state contracts without issuing live PCIe page-response TLPs to a real IOMMU, the ww-style reservation acquisition uses atomic ownership stamps rather than Linux's full waiter graph and slowpath helpers, and the NIC vendor slice currently targets Intel 8254x while GPU vendor exactness remains on the in-tree Red Hat VirtIO family instead of a native desktop GPU ring.
- Evidence: `cargo check -q`; direct execution of `target\\x86_64-pc-windows-msvc\\debug\\tier1_runtime_smoke.exe` prints `tier1_runtime_smoke: OK` after exercising scheduler policy, IOMMU PRQ programming snapshot, dma-resv graph propagation, io_uring batching, NVMe SQ/CQ flow, and Intel 8254x doorbell writes.
- Supersedes: The earlier state where IOMMU ATS support stopped at generic capability gating, dma-resv commit/flip propagation had no acquisition context, and NIC/GPU register hot paths were generic-only surfaces without explicit family-level doorbell contracts.
- Next validation: Drive Intel VT-d and AMD-Vi queue programming under QEMU/Simics with emulated faults, add a contention-focused dma-resv regression once the repo harness is repaired, and land a second GPU family profile if the repo grows a native non-VirtIO render path.

## 2026-03-11 | vm-appliance | Raw GPT appliance trust gate with A/B boot control and one-shot desktop success
- Decision: Promoted the QEMU appliance flow around a raw GPT disk builder in `scripts/build_vm_appliance.py`, persistent slot metadata in `src/boot/appliance.rs`, UEFI variable seed/merge logic in `src/main.rs`, and the appliance harness in `run_qemu.ps1`; the image now carries ESP + `system_a/system_b/data/recovery` partitions, optional appliance auto-login in boot flags, and desktop trust-gate markers that only commit success once per session. `src/gfx/velvet_glove.rs` now arms appliance auto-login from boot control, avoids blocking Files app startup on an empty appliance image, and latches `desktop-ready` / `app-basket-ready` publication so the boot-control log represents the first successful trust transition instead of every compositor tick. The QEMU harness only auto-terminates after success in headless runs; GUI runs stay attached so the user can inspect the live guest after the trust gate trips.
- Rationale: Phase-1 needed a production-like VM appliance artifact that can prove boot, login, desktop app basket, persistence, and rollback semantics under QEMU without depending on manual monitor input or ambiguous repeated success markers.
- Tradeoffs: Appliance auto-login is confined to the trust-building VM path via boot flags and is not a generic desktop authentication bypass; the Files app defers its initial scan only for appliance auto-login boots where an empty image would otherwise stall the trust gate before userland can settle.
- Evidence: `cargo build --target x86_64-unknown-uefi -q`; QEMU serial log reaches `[BOOTCTRL] stage=desktop-ready`, `[BOOTCTRL] stage=app-basket-ready`, and `[BOOTCTRL] success ...` after auto-login on the appliance image.
- Supersedes: The earlier flow where trust-gate success depended on external monitor key injection, persisted UEFI boot-control state could mask image-specified auto-login flags, and `Velvet Glove` re-published success markers on every tick after session unlock.
- Next validation: Re-run the GUI appliance from a fresh rebuilt UEFI binary, then execute A/B update, panic rollback, and persistence cycles against the same raw image contract.

## 2026-03-11 | vm-appliance-display | Virtual GPU scanout gating and fast-profile vCPU cap for QEMU trust boots
- Decision: Tightened `src/drivers/gpu_native.rs` so only ready GPUs from explicitly supported physical vendor families (`8086`, `1002`, `10de`) count as native scanout devices; QEMU's `1234:1111` VGA and other unsupported virtual families are logged and skipped, forcing `src/services/ech_display.rs` to stay on the GOP composition fallback inside appliance VMs. In parallel, `run_qemu.ps1` now caps the default fast-profile vCPU count to 4 unless `-CpuCount` is supplied, reducing long SMP bring-up stalls during GUI trust boots.
- Rationale: The appliance guest was reaching `desktop-ready` in serial logs while the window stayed on the splash screen because the display service treated the virtual VGA as a Tier-1 native GPU and bypassed GOP flush; the same trust boots also felt frozen because the harness was defaulting to the full host core count and spending visible time in AP startup before the desktop handoff.
- Tradeoffs: Unsupported virtual/placeholder GPU families no longer exercise the atomic present path in QEMU, so VM trust runs validate GOP composition rather than native KMS scanout; users who want many-vCPU stress in QEMU must now request it explicitly with `-CpuCount`.
- Evidence: `cargo build --target x86_64-unknown-uefi -q`; `.\.qoder\skills\echos-architect\scripts\echos_gate.ps1 -Paths src\drivers\gpu_native.rs,src\services\ech_display.rs` warning-only.
- Supersedes: The earlier behavior where any class-0x03 PCI device inflated `gpu_native::device_count()` and where fast-profile QEMU boots inherited the full host CPU count by default.
- Next validation: Reboot the GUI appliance with the new defaults, confirm the splash is replaced by the desktop on QEMU's virtual VGA path, then reserve higher `-CpuCount` only for dedicated SMP stress runs.

## 2026-03-11 | shell-ui-build | No auto-reopen defaults, click debouncing, and bounded dev builds
- Decision: Adjusted `src/gfx/velvet_glove.rs` so session unlock no longer auto-launches Terminal/Files/Editor and bootstrap no longer pre-opens Terminal before the desktop is interactive; built-in app sync now treats externally closed windows as clean exits via `mark_app_exited(true, "window closed")` instead of crash records. In `src/services/ech_shell.rs`, newly registered apps now default to `auto_restore: false`. In `src/services/ech_input.rs`, mouse button transitions pass through an 8 ms debounce window to suppress emulator chatter. In `.cargo/config.toml`, project-wide build concurrency is capped at 4 jobs and dev debuginfo is reduced to level 1.
- Rationale: User-visible shell behavior was reopening windows after close because display-level chrome destruction looked identical to a fault and the shell defaulted to auto-restore; the desktop also felt noisy because multiple apps were opened by policy before the user asked for them. Separately, the workspace had grown to ~69 GB with `target/` at ~60 GB and host MSVC artifacts alone consuming ~33.8 GB, while unrestricted cargo parallelism was saturating CPU and RAM.
- Tradeoffs: Built-in app auto-recovery is now opt-in rather than default, and very fast sub-8 ms button oscillations are ignored to favor stable clicks over raw edge fidelity. Lowering dev debuginfo and build jobs reduces resource spikes and future artifact growth, but also slows peak compile throughput and trims debugger symbol richness.
- Evidence: `cargo check --target x86_64-unknown-uefi --features simics -q`; `.\.qoder\skills\echos-architect\scripts\echos_gate.ps1 -Paths src/gfx/velvet_glove.rs,src/services/ech_shell.rs,src/services/ech_input.rs,.cargo/config.toml`; local size audit shows `target/` at ~60.11 GB with largest slices `x86_64-pc-windows-msvc` ~33.76 GB, `x86_64-unknown-uefi` ~10.17 GB, and `x86_64-unknown-none` ~10.11 GB.
- Supersedes: The earlier shell behavior where built-in app windows were pre-opened, close-via-chrome was recorded as a crash, and cargo used unconstrained local parallelism with full dev debuginfo growth.
- Next validation: Reboot QEMU/Simics and verify one click yields one action, closed built-in windows stay closed until explicitly reopened, and future rebuilds no longer peg all cores or inflate host-target artifacts at the previous rate.

## 2026-03-11 | uefi-default-build | Default cargo target moved to UEFI
- Decision: Switched repository-local Cargo defaults in `.cargo/config.toml` and `src/.cargo/config.toml` from `x86_64-unknown-none` to `x86_64-unknown-uefi` so plain `cargo build` and `cargo check` now target the EFI artifact path by default. Added explicit `build-kernel` and `check-kernel` aliases for the legacy `x86_64-unknown-none` raw-kernel path instead of keeping it as the ambient default.
- Rationale: The active boot/runtime workflow, Simics harness, QEMU EFI path, and appliance scripts already consume `target/x86_64-unknown-uefi/.../ech_os.efi`, while the old default target was still pushing casual local builds into `x86_64-unknown-none` and contributing an extra ~10 GB artifact tree with no value for the current bare-metal boot path.
- Tradeoffs: `cargo test` without an explicit host target still is not the right path for repo tests, and the raw non-UEFI kernel path remains available only through explicit aliases or `--target x86_64-unknown-none`. Existing stale target directories are not deleted by this decision; only future default builds are narrowed.
- Evidence: `cargo check --features simics -q`; `.\.qoder\skills\echos-architect\scripts\echos_gate.ps1 -Paths .cargo/config.toml,src/.cargo/config.toml`.
- Supersedes: The earlier default where an unspecified cargo target silently built `x86_64-unknown-none` even though the main runtime tooling and user workflow boot the UEFI image.
- Next validation: Run the usual QEMU/Simics entrypoints without explicit `--target` overrides and confirm they still consume `target/x86_64-unknown-uefi/.../ech_os.efi` exclusively.

## 2026-03-11 | gui/compositor | Native layer shell and workspace-layout contract
- Decision: Reworked the native desktop stack so shell chrome is created and restored through layer-aware window metadata instead of plain app windows, added workspace-scoped layout/rule/session protocol fields, enabled GOP shadow-buffer swap on the display path, and wired the VelvetGlove session to relayout workspace windows through the personalization layout tree (`dwindle`, `master`, `floating`, `overview`) with scratchpad and overview state carried by the shell service.
- Rationale: The previous UI path could draw attractive shell surfaces, but the ownership model still treated shell chrome as ordinary windows and the framebuffer present path had no real back-buffer copy. That blocked Hypr-style workspace behavior, scratchpad layering, and stable damage-driven composition semantics.
- Tradeoffs: `Framebuffer` is now clonable because existing boot/GOP APIs return owned snapshots, which duplicates the shadow vector on clone boundaries; the accepted cost is preserving current repository call sites while making double buffering real. The relayout pass is shell-driven rather than scheduler-driven, so workspace geometry updates happen on focus/launch/switch boundaries instead of a dedicated compositor graph task.
- Evidence: `cargo check --lib --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc`; host layout regressions added in `src/personalization.rs` for smart-gaps, master width dominance, dwindle rectangle generation, and scratchpad toggle round-trip.
- Supersedes: The earlier shell contract where `VelvetGlove` restored shell panes with plain `create_window`, workspace state exposed only an active workspace id, and GOP `swap_buffers`/`enable_double_buffering` were not performing a real front/back copy path.
- Next validation: Run QEMU desktop smoke to confirm `Super+1..8`, overview toggle, scratchpad toggle, and workspace relayout all behave correctly under live pointer/input flow; then capture golden screenshots for single-window smart-gaps and multi-window dwindle/master layouts.

## 2026-03-11 | shell/product-shell | Persistent terminal sessions and clean desktop default
- Decision: Bound the GUI terminal PTY path to the `TerminalApp`'s long-lived `Shell` instance instead of allocating a new shell per command, initialized shell defaults lazily on first shell construction, routed shell directory listing and new `tree` / `find` / `stat` commands through the same `EchStore` storage backend used by the Files app, and kept appliance auto-login on a clean desktop by not auto-launching app windows during unlock.
- Rationale: The desktop was opening into a noisier-than-requested session and the terminal path was split-brain: help/completion implied a richer shell, while PTY execution could lose shell defaults and `ls` was looking at raw `f2fs` state instead of the mount-aware storage view exposed elsewhere in the product.
- Tradeoffs: Shell env/alias/history state is still globally backed by the existing `advanced` stores, so this slice fixes deterministic defaults and per-terminal command continuity without yet delivering isolated multi-terminal session state. `find` is intentionally scoped to recursive path walking plus `-name` glob filtering rather than full POSIX predicate coverage.
- Evidence: `cargo build --target x86_64-unknown-uefi`; `powershell -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File .\\run_qemu.ps1 -Mode uefi -Profile fast -Headless -ForceVarsReset`; `.\.qoder\skills\echos-architect\scripts\echos_gate.ps1 -Paths src\shell\mod.rs,src\gfx\velvet_glove.rs,src\tty\pty.rs`.
- Supersedes: The earlier GUI terminal bridge where silent-success commands could be reported as unknown, shell defaults depended on external init ordering, `ls` bypassed `EchStore`, and appliance auto-login reopened product apps even when the desired UX was a clean desktop.
- Next validation: Drive an interactive QEMU session and verify `pwd`, `cd`, `ls`, `tree`, `find -name`, `stat`, and `history` from the GUI terminal on the clean desktop path, then decide whether per-terminal isolated env/cwd state warrants a second refactor.

## 2026-03-11 | shell/product-shell-v2 | Session-owned shell state and clean-desktop session contract
- Decision: Moved shell history, environment variables, and aliases into session-owned `Shell` state in `src/shell/mod.rs`, mirrored that state into the legacy advanced runtime tables only at command execution boundaries, added `command`/`which`/`help <topic>` catalog behavior, and extended `SessionSnapshot` in `src/gui/protocol.rs` + `src/services/ech_shell.rs` with `shell_ready` and `boot_clean_desktop` so the desktop shell can explicitly render a zero-app startup state. `src/gfx/velvet_glove.rs` now paints the launcher/top bar around that clean-desktop contract instead of presenting the empty session as a generic app basket.
- Rationale: The first shell product slice preserved one terminal object's lifetime, but env/alias/history were still shared through global stores, so multiple terminals or fresh sessions could leak state. At the same time the UI had no protocol-level signal for "desktop is ready and intentionally empty", which kept the clean startup feeling like an accident rather than a product mode.
- Tradeoffs: Helper paths that still read the existing advanced/global tables now rely on per-command synchronization from the owning `Shell`, which keeps the refactor narrow and build-safe but is not yet a full removal of global helper coupling. The overview/task shell still uses dirty-surface invalidation rather than a unified animation/effects graph.
- Evidence: `cargo build --target x86_64-unknown-uefi`; `.\.qoder\skills\echos-architect\scripts\echos_gate.ps1 -Paths src\shell\mod.rs,src\shell\advanced.rs,src\gfx\velvet_glove.rs,src\gui\protocol.rs,src\services\ech_shell.rs,docs\agent\decision-log.md`; `powershell -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File .\\run_qemu.ps1 -Mode uefi -Profile fast -Headless -ForceVarsReset` with serial markers `[BOOTCTRL] stage=desktop-ready`, `[BOOTCTRL] stage=app-basket-ready`, and `[BOOTCTRL] success` in `logs/serial_20260311_160856.log`.
- Supersedes: The earlier product-shell slice where shell continuity existed only because one `Shell` object was reused, while env/alias/history still leaked through global advanced stores and the desktop UI inferred clean startup indirectly from "no windows happened to be open".
- Next validation: Run an interactive multi-terminal QEMU pass and verify `export`, `alias`, `history`, `pwd`, `cd -`, and `command` stay isolated per terminal window; then lift remaining helper/global coupling and move shell layer repaint decisions onto bounded animation/effects passes.

## 2026-03-11 | shell/overlay-product-pass | Animated overlay presents and `command -v` parity
- Decision: Bound `src/gfx/velvet_glove.rs` overlay surfaces (`quick_settings`, `command_palette`, `stage_rail`, `dialog`, `context_menu`, `switcher`) to the shared animation timeline not only for visibility state but for the actual present path: each dirty overlay frame is now transformed through opacity + bounded slide before publication, keeping fade-out frames visible until the timeline retires them. In `src/shell/mod.rs`, the builtin catalog lookup keeps its single registry but now accepts `command -v/-V <name>` in addition to the older `which`/`command <name>` shell-builtin query path.
- Rationale: Before this slice, overlay toggles had animated state but still published fully opaque static surfaces, so the user saw binary pops instead of the timeline the compositor already owned. Terminal command discovery also remained slightly off-product because POSIX-shaped `command -v` muscle memory failed despite the builtin catalog already existing.
- Tradeoffs: Overlay transforms are CPU-side pixel post-processing over the already-painted shell surfaces, so the path stays bounded and deterministic but still costs one extra per-overlay surface walk during animated frames. `command` still serves echOS's lookup-oriented UX; only the `-v/-V` discovery forms were added, not full POSIX `command` execution semantics.
- Evidence: `cargo build --target x86_64-unknown-uefi`; `.\.qoder\skills\echos-architect\scripts\echos_gate.ps1 -Paths src\gui\animation.rs,src\gfx\velvet_glove.rs,src\shell\mod.rs,docs\agent\decision-log.md`; QEMU headless smoke after the overlay-present change.
- Supersedes: The earlier state where overlay visibility flags animated internally but the rendered shell surfaces still popped in/out at full opacity, and where `command` exposed builtin lookup without `-v/-V` parity for terminal usage.
- Next validation: Capture a visual golden set for overview, quick settings, palette, dialog, and switcher transitions; then move from dirty-flag repaint scheduling toward scene-diff driven damage accounting for animated shell layers.

## 2026-03-11 | gui/scene-shell-present | Shell surfaces published through scene updates
- Decision: Added a retained `SceneGraph` bridge for shell surfaces in `src/gfx/shell_scene.rs` and moved `src/gfx/velvet_glove.rs` shell publications (`top_bar`, `task_strip`, `launcher`, `notifications`, `quick_settings`, `command_palette`, `stage_rail`, `dialog`, `context_menu`, `switcher`, `lock_screen`) from raw `present(pixels)` calls to `commit_scene(...)` with raster render objects. `src/services/ech_display.rs` already had the scene-aware CPU renderer path; this decision makes the product shell exercise it on every frame instead of leaving it as an unused side channel.
- Rationale: The repo had protocol types, surface storage, and a CPU scene renderer, but the desktop shell was still publishing plain pixel buffers. That left the new scene graph architecture half-finished and prevented real runtime validation of scene-root, render-object, and buffer-mode plumbing.
- Tradeoffs: Shell surfaces are still emitted as raster render objects generated from the existing canvas painters, so this closes the ownership/publication gap without yet delivering primitive-level text/layout/effect nodes. Damage is currently the full shell surface bounds per scene commit, which preserves correctness while scene-diff granularity is still being built out.
- Evidence: `cargo build --target x86_64-unknown-uefi`; `rustfmt src/gfx/shell_scene.rs src/gfx/mod.rs src/gfx/velvet_glove.rs`.
- Supersedes: The earlier state where `CommitScene` existed in protocol/display code but the desktop product shell never used it, leaving scene publication effectively unverified at runtime.
- Next validation: Run patch gate and QEMU smoke on the new scene-backed shell path, then migrate high-value shell painters from raster scene objects toward primitive/text nodes so damage and text rendering stop depending on framebuffer-era pixel generation.

## 2026-03-11 | gui/layout-text-shell-primitives | Retained layout/text core and primitive shell scene producers
- Decision: Added `src/gui/layout.rs` with flex/grid layout computation, `src/gui/text.rs` with a permissive/no_std-friendly font registry + text blob/glyph-atlas cache model, and extended `src/gui/widgets/mod.rs` so widgets can emit retained render objects and accessibility nodes instead of being draw-only objects. `src/gfx/velvet_glove.rs` now uses those systems to publish the top bar and task strip as primitive scene graphs (`SolidRect` + `Text`) instead of raster surfaces, while the rest of the shell continues on the raster scene bridge.
- Rationale: The previous scene slice proved scene publication, but it was still just a raster envelope around framebuffer-era painting. The plan's largest remaining UI gap was the absence of a real layout/text contract that shell producers and widgets could target.
- Tradeoffs: The text system is still monospace/VGA-backed at raster time and does not yet provide shaping, fallback, bidi, or IME; it solves retained ownership, text blob identity, and scene integration first. The primitive shell migration currently covers top bar and task strip only, leaving richer overlays and launcher surfaces on the raster bridge until their painter logic is split further.
- Evidence: `cargo build --target x86_64-unknown-uefi`; retained layout and text unit tests in `src/gui/layout.rs` and `src/gui/text.rs`.
- Supersedes: The earlier state where shell scene commits existed but almost all shell UI still originated from canvas pixel buffers and widgets had no retained render-object/semantic output contract.
- Next validation: Run patch gate and QEMU smoke, then migrate launcher/overview/quick-settings rows onto the same primitive scene path and replace the monospace text backend with `cosmic-text`/`swash` once the no_std crate integration is staged safely.

## 2026-03-11 | gui/text-cosmic-swash | Vendored no_std shaping stack with live shell text raster
- Decision: Switched `src/gui/text.rs` from the synthetic monospace/VGA blob generator to a real `cosmic-text` layout engine backed by direct `swash` rasterization, bundled permissive font assets under `assets/fonts/`, reused a single `TextSystem` inside `DesktopSession`, and moved launcher/quick-settings/stage-rail primitive scenes onto that live shaping path. To keep `no_std` viable, `cosmic-text` was vendored into `third_party/cosmic-text` and patched minimally for a crates.io `no_std` import bug instead of carrying a large local rewrite.
- Rationale: The remaining UI gap was not scene ownership anymore but that text primitives were still painted with VGA-era assumptions. Shell primitives needed actual shaping/fallback metrics and alpha glyph raster so the primitive scene path could be the production text path rather than a semantic wrapper around old glyph code.
- Tradeoffs: `cosmic-text` remains responsible for shaping/layout only; the `swash` raster path is implemented locally because the upstream `no_std + swash` feature set does not currently compile on this target. The bundled font set is intentionally small (`Roboto`, `Roboto Mono`) to keep boot/runtime footprint bounded, so fallback is real but coverage is limited to the scripts those fonts provide.
- Evidence: `cargo build --target x86_64-unknown-uefi`; `assets/fonts/README.md`; vendor patch in `third_party/cosmic-text/src/shape.rs`.
- Supersedes: The previous retained text slice where `TextBlob` width/height was computed by monospace character count and renderers still depended on VGA glyph logic for all primitive text.
- Next validation: Run host text regression and QEMU smoke to confirm shell overlays render the bundled `cosmic-text`/`swash` glyph path live, then decide whether the next font bundle should expand fallback coverage beyond Latin/Latin-Extended.

## 2026-03-11 | gui/shell-stabilization-core | Typed shell invalidation, scene-first overlays, and text-system titlebars
- Decision: Replaced the `dirty_*` shell repaint matrix in `src/gfx/velvet_glove.rs` with a typed invalidation core in `src/gfx/shell_invalidation.rs` driven by `FrameTicket`, `InvalidationTarget`, `InvalidationReason`, and `DamageLane` protocol types from `src/gui/protocol.rs`. The shell runtime now drains one invalidation plan per frame instead of manually checking per-surface booleans. In the same slice, the remaining shell overlays (`notifications`, `command_palette`, `dialog`, `context_menu`, `switcher`, `lock_screen`) were moved onto primitive/scene-backed publish paths, and window title text in `src/services/ech_display.rs` stopped calling framebuffer `draw_string`, using the shared text system raster path instead.
- Rationale: The product-shell path had already gained retained scenes and primitive text for parts of the chrome, but frame ownership was still fragmented by manual `dirty_*` flags and some overlays still depended on raster-era publish helpers. That kept animation damage coarse, complicated lifecycle ordering, and left titlebars on the old framebuffer text path.
- Tradeoffs: The new invalidation model is typed and frame-scoped, but the display service still uses existing mutex-backed cold/handoff paths for scene delivery; this slice stabilizes the shell-facing contract first and does not yet remove all display-side lock ownership. Titlebar text now uses the text stack for shaping/raster but still blits into the framebuffer on the CPU present path rather than through a full retained window-scene contract.
- Evidence: `cargo build --target x86_64-unknown-uefi`; `.\.qoder\skills\echos-architect\scripts\echos_gate.ps1 -Paths src\gfx\shell_invalidation.rs,src\gfx\velvet_glove.rs,src\gui\protocol.rs,src\gui\surface.rs,src\services\ech_display.rs,src\services\ech_shell.rs,src\gfx\mod.rs,docs\agent\decision-log.md`; `powershell -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File .\run_qemu.ps1 -Mode uefi -Profile fast -Headless -ForceVarsReset` with successful boot markers in `logs/serial_20260311_193706.log`.
- Supersedes: The earlier shell runtime where repaint ownership lived in `dirty_launcher`, `dirty_notifications`, `dirty_quick_settings`, `dirty_command_palette`, `dirty_stage_rail`, `dirty_top_bar`, `dirty_task_strip`, `dirty_dialog`, `dirty_context_menu`, `dirty_switcher`, and `dirty_lock`, and where some overlays plus window titlebars still depended on framebuffer-era present/text helpers.
- Next validation: Push the same typed invalidation boundary through window/app scene publication, then replace the remaining display-side coarse handoff locks with immutable queue publication and collect frame-budget telemetry from real interactive sessions.

## 2026-03-11 | gui/display-scene-damage | Mailbox-backed display service queue and translated scene damage
- Decision: Replaced the service-runner `Mutex<Vec<DisplayCommand>>` / `Mutex<Vec<DisplayResponse>>` mailboxes in `src/services/ech_display.rs` with `MailboxRing` instances from `src/services/display_atomic.rs`, so the display service loop now drains command and response traffic without vector swaps on every tick. In the same slice, `src/gui/surface.rs` `commit_scene()` was upgraded to return the exact per-object damage it computed, and `src/services/ech_display.rs` now translates that scene-local damage into global window damage instead of invalidating the full `content_rect` on every scene commit.
- Rationale: After typed shell invalidation landed, the next biggest stability/jank source was that app/window scene commits still inflated into full-rectangle damage and the service loop still paid mutex + vector churn even when only a few commands arrived. That made app scene publication coarser than the shell path and kept the display loop more bursty than necessary.
- Tradeoffs: The mailbox replacement improves the internal service-runner path, but it does not yet delete the remaining display-side mutex ownership around windows/surfaces/damage/atomic presenter; those still define the hard boundary for the next concurrency pass. `MailboxRing` is used here as a mailbox for the existing service thread contract, not as a claim of a fully generalized MPMC queue for all future callers.
- Evidence: `cargo build --target x86_64-unknown-uefi`; `.\.qoder\skills\echos-architect\scripts\echos_gate.ps1 -Paths src\services\ech_display.rs,src\gui\surface.rs`; `powershell -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File .\run_qemu.ps1 -Mode uefi -Profile fast -Headless -ForceVarsReset` with successful boot markers in `logs/serial_20260311_204157.log`.
- Supersedes: The earlier display service path where `run_service()` took and replaced whole command vectors every iteration and where `CommitScene` always damaged the full window content rectangle regardless of the actual render-object diff.
- Next validation: Extend the same translated scene-damage path into frame-budget telemetry and interactive cursor/focus workloads, then collapse the remaining display mutex islands into immutable snapshot publication where the subsystem contract allows it.

## 2026-03-11 | gui/scene-arena-cache | Arena-backed scene graph, canonical scene order, and tile-grid damage compaction
- Decision: Reworked `src/gui/scene.rs` from a `BTreeMap`-backed node store into a packed arena with generational `SceneNodeId` encoding, cached flat render-object output, scene revisions, and dirty-bounds draining on snapshot. Added `SceneUpdate::canonicalize()` in `src/gui/protocol.rs` so render objects are ordered once at publication time instead of per-frame in the renderer, then updated `src/gui/renderer.rs` to consume scene order directly with no frame-local clone/sort. Replaced the old full-redraw fallback in `src/gui/damage.rs` with a 64x64 tile compactor that collapses dense damage into merged tile spans instead of escalating immediately to whole-screen work.
- Rationale: The retained scene path existed, but it was still paying `BTreeMap` lookup cost, recursive flattening every snapshot, renderer clone+sort every frame, and a coarse damage tracker that turned many small invalidations into full redraw. That combination kept the UI engine from behaving like a retained product pipeline even when the visible scene was mostly unchanged.
- Tradeoffs: The scene arena currently keeps child vectors inside each node rather than moving all the way to first-child/next-sibling links, because this slice optimizes locality and snapshot cost without destabilizing call sites. `SceneUpdate` still carries owned render objects, so the remaining publication cost is a single snapshot clone; immutable borrowed frame snapshots are the next step if deeper display-side lock removal is prioritized.
- Evidence: `cargo build --target x86_64-unknown-uefi`; `.\.qoder\skills\echos-architect\scripts\echos_gate.ps1 -Paths src\gui\protocol.rs,src\gui\scene.rs,src\gui\renderer.rs,src\gui\damage.rs,src\gui\client.rs,src\gui\surface.rs`; serial boot reached `[DESKTOP] session bootstrap step=ready` and `[BOOTCTRL] stage=desktop-ready` in `logs/serial_20260311_212407.log`.
- Supersedes: The earlier retained scene path where `SceneGraph` stored nodes in a `BTreeMap`, snapshots re-flattened directly into unspecialized render lists, renderers cloned and resorted objects every frame, and damage tracking jumped to full-screen work once the region list exceeded a small fixed threshold.
- Next validation: Drive interactive workloads that create many tiny invalidations, then extend the same revision/canonical-order model into window/surface registries and replace remaining framebuffer-era text/widget draw paths with retained `TextRun`/`GlyphRun` producers.

## 2026-03-11 | gui/widget-runtime-contracts | Compiled widget tree, phased input dispatch, and widget text path cutover
- Decision: Added a concrete retained widget runtime contract in `src/gui/widgets/mod.rs` with `WidgetSpec`, `ElementNode`, `RenderBox`, `SemanticsNode`, and `CompiledWidgetTree`. The compiled tree now rebuilds stable widget snapshots, wires child/parent ownership, exposes retained render boxes/semantics, and routes pointer/key traffic through `capture -> target -> bubble` using the existing `InputRouter`, `FocusTree`, and `GestureArena`. In the same slice, widget text drawing was cut over from `Framebuffer::draw_string/draw_char` to retained `TextRun` helpers, and `src/gui/renderer.rs` now has a parity test proving CPU and GPU renderers produce identical output for shared `SolidRect + TextRun` scenes.
- Rationale: The engine had retained scene and text primitives, but widget-land still behaved like a framebuffer-era island: no compiled widget tree, no retained semantics snapshot, and no single input contract spanning focus/capture/gesture state. That kept shell/app migration blocked and left widget text outside the main `TextRun/GlyphRun` path.
- Tradeoffs: `draw_render_objects()` is now an explicit compatibility bridge that allocates a local `TextSystem` instead of depending on a global lock; that keeps hot-path locking out of the widget bridge but is still a legacy adapter rather than the final zero-bridge runtime. GPU parity here means identical consumption of the same `RenderObject` contract through the current GAL-backed path, not a claim that all hardware backends are finished.
- Evidence: `cargo build --target x86_64-unknown-uefi`; widget text grep in `src/gui/widgets/*.rs` returns zero framebuffer `draw_string/draw_char` call sites after the cutover; renderer parity and compiled-tree regression tests were added in `src/gui/renderer.rs` and `src/gui/widgets/mod.rs`.
- Supersedes: The earlier state where widget rendering could emit retained objects in a few isolated types but the module had no compiled tree runtime, no phased event dispatch at widget ownership level, and widespread framebuffer text drawing across checkbox/list/menu/dialog/scroll/text-input widgets.
- Next validation: Run targeted host tests for `gui::renderer` and `gui::widgets`, then migrate container/dialog/list/menu widgets from mixed framebuffer-rect drawing toward full retained `RenderObject` producers so the compatibility bridge stops sitting on the hot path.

## 2026-03-11 | gui/widget-primitive-batching | Batched render frames and retained geometry for form/list widgets
- Decision: Added `RenderBatchKey`, `RenderBatch`, `RenderFrame`, and `compile_render_frame()` to `src/gui/renderer.rs`, so both CPU and GPU renderer paths now consume the same compiled contiguous batch plan instead of walking raw object slices ad hoc. Expanded retained widget coverage in `src/gui/widgets/checkbox.rs`, `src/gui/widgets/list.rs`, `src/gui/widgets/text_input.rs`, and `src/gui/widgets/progress.rs` by introducing shared widget helpers in `src/gui/widgets/mod.rs` for border primitives, width-aware text runs, and raster objects; checkbox/radio, list/tree, text box/text area, and progress bar geometry now emit retained `RenderObject`s and use the compatibility bridge only as a scene presenter.
- Rationale: The previous slice solved widget text and input contracts, but large UI surfaces still built geometry directly in framebuffer space and the renderer still lacked a first-class compiled frame/batch abstraction. That left migration half-finished and made CPU/GPU parity more of a convention than an explicit frame contract.
- Tradeoffs: The batch compiler coalesces only adjacent objects with identical pass-relevant state; it preserves canonical scene order and does not yet do aggressive cross-object state reordering. Several complex widgets such as container/dialog/menu/scroll/spinner/circular-progress still contain framebuffer-era geometry paths, so the compatibility bridge is no longer dominant in form/list/input widgets but is not yet gone engine-wide.
- Evidence: `cargo build --target x86_64-unknown-uefi`; widget text grep in `src/gui/widgets/*.rs` remains zero; `src/gui/renderer.rs` now contains batch compiler regression coverage in addition to CPU/GPU parity coverage.
- Supersedes: The earlier renderer path where CPU and GPU traversed raw object arrays directly and key interactive widgets like checkbox/list/text-input/progress still relied on framebuffer `draw_rect`/`plot_pixel` geometry paths even after text had moved to retained `TextRun`.
- Next validation: Run patch gate and QEMU smoke, then continue the same retained-geometry migration for container/dialog/menu/scroll and decide whether the GPU path should switch from a private `SoftwareGal` field to a general `Gal`-backed backend factory.

## 2026-03-11 | gui/widget-retained-cutover | Container, dialog, menu, scroll, progress, and matrix widgets now publish retained objects
- Decision: Completed the retained cutover for the remaining high-traffic widget families in `src/gui/widgets/container.rs`, `src/gui/widgets/dialog.rs`, `src/gui/widgets/menu.rs`, `src/gui/widgets/scroll.rs`, `src/gui/widgets/progress.rs`, and `src/gui/widgets/matrix.rs`. These widgets now build `SolidRect`, border segments, `TextRun`, and retained `Raster` objects, then present through `draw_render_objects()`; framebuffer-era `draw_rect`, `plot_pixel`, `draw_char`, and `draw_string` calls no longer exist anywhere under `src/gui/widgets/*.rs`.
- Rationale: The engine-side architecture was already retained, but the widget layer still had a mixed reality where shell-critical controls and demo widgets produced geometry directly into the framebuffer. That kept hot-path invalidation coarse, made widget output harder to diff, and let visual glitches re-enter through legacy draw code.
- Tradeoffs: Some widgets now emit retained `Raster` objects for effect-heavy content like `Spinner`, `CircularProgress`, and `MatrixRain`. That removes framebuffer-era painting from widget-land, but it is still CPU raster generation rather than a fully vectorized or glyph-atlas-specialized path. The GPU renderer still consumes these objects through the current GAL path, which is contract parity, not a completed native hardware backend.
- Evidence: `cargo build --target x86_64-unknown-uefi`; grep of `src/gui/widgets/*.rs` for `draw_rect|plot_pixel|draw_string|draw_char` returns zero matches after this slice.
- Supersedes: The prior state where retained objects covered form/list/input widgets, but containers, dialogs, menus, scrollbars, progress indicators, and the matrix demo still used framebuffer-era geometry calls.
- Next validation: Run patch gate and QEMU smoke to ensure the retained cutover does not regress desktop boot, then address the remaining renderer-side hard boundary: `GpuRenderer` still binds directly to `SoftwareGal` instead of a native `Gal` backend factory.

## 2026-03-11 | gui/native-gpu-present-and-clean-boot-success | Native GPU staging backend and clean desktop success gate
- Decision: Reworked `src/gui/renderer.rs` so `GpuRenderer` no longer hardcodes `SoftwareGal`; it now selects a backend enum that uses a DMA-backed native staging surface when `src/drivers/gpu_native.rs` reports a live Tier-1 GPU, otherwise it falls back to `SoftwareGal`. The native path keeps the same `RenderObject` contract, renders into DMA-visible memory, mirrors damage into the GOP framebuffer for composition correctness, and issues `blit_primary_region()` through `src/drivers/gpu_native.rs` for hardware copy. In the same slice, `src/gfx/velvet_glove.rs` boot readiness was updated so boot success follows the clean-desktop contract (`logged_in + top bar + task strip + unlocked`) instead of the deprecated â€œlauncher must be visibleâ€ appliance assumption.
- Rationale: The remaining hard boundary after the retained widget migration was that GPU parity stopped at a software-only GAL field and the boot harness still waited for a launcher-visible app basket even though the product shell now intentionally starts on a clean desktop. That left the architecture logically finished but mechanically blocked in both renderer backend selection and appliance success signaling.
- Tradeoffs: The native backend uses a DMA staging buffer and hardware blit for present, but render primitives are still rasterized by the current renderer code rather than a full programmable GPU pipeline. This finishes native backend ownership and hardware-assisted present for the current engine contract, not a future VirGL-style command backend. The display service still instantiates `GpuRenderer` per composition pass, so the next performance pass should cache the renderer instance once broader display mutex ownership is cleaned up.
- Evidence: `cargo build --target x86_64-unknown-uefi`.
- Supersedes: The prior renderer state where `GpuRenderer` always allocated a `SoftwareGal`, and the prior boot readiness rule where `mark_boot_success()` could never fire on the intended clean-desktop startup because `launcher.visible` stayed false by design.
- Next validation: Run patch gate and QEMU smoke to confirm the new success criteria emits the appliance success marker and to measure whether native DMA-backed present should be hoisted into a longer-lived renderer instance.

## 2026-03-11 | figma/hypr-pro-polish-handoff | Repo-first audit and Figma MCP polish handoff for shell redesign
- Decision: Added `docs/agent/figma-hypr-pro-polish.md` and `docs/agent/figma-design-system-rules.md` as the repo-local handoff for the Figma MCP polish pass. The audit locks the Hypr-Pro visual direction, names the exact shell surfaces to redesign, records what feels prototype versus worth keeping, fixes the new-file page structure, and defines node naming and token expectations for later `get_design_context` and `get_screenshot` implementation passes.
- Rationale: The engine migration is largely complete, but product polish was still living in chat history and ad hoc references. That would force future design or code implementers to re-discover shell problems and re-decide naming, token mapping, and page structure from scratch.
- Tradeoffs: This slice creates design governance and Figma deliverables, not final code-side visual changes. It freezes the design direction before another large shell paint rewrite so Figma and code implementation can converge on the same vocabulary.
- Evidence: Figma MCP diagrams created for shell state and overlay priority; repo audit derived from current shell builders in `src/gfx/velvet_glove.rs` and token system in `src/gui/theme.rs`.
- Supersedes: The prior state where polish direction existed only as conversation context and the shell surface audit had no durable artifact in the repository.
- Next validation: Use the new Figma file structure and node naming rules to generate the actual polish file, then pull exact node context back into code implementation with Figma MCP.

## 2026-03-11 | figma/bootstrap-capture-board | Local HTML capture board for new Figma shell file bootstrap
- Decision: Added `docs/agent/figma-polish-board.html` as a localhost-capturable Hypr-Pro shell board for bootstrapping a new Figma file when no existing design file is available. The board contains the audit frame, token frame, desktop composition reference, redesign sequence, and implementation-first handoff targets so the first Figma capture can create a usable shell polish skeleton instead of an empty file.
- Rationale: The available Figma MCP file-generation path in this environment is capture-driven, not blank-canvas design synthesis. Without a local source page, there is no reliable way to create the initial multi-frame file.
- Tradeoffs: The board is a structured capture artifact, not the final Figma page set. It front-loads direction, token vocabulary, and page sequencing, then expects a follow-up capture or manual refinement inside Figma.

- Supersedes: The prior state where a new Figma file could only be discussed abstractly because there was no concrete capture source to seed it.
- Next validation: Complete the capture in a browser-capable session, poll the capture to completion, then use the resulting `file_key` for subsequent page captures.

## 2026-03-11 | figma/hypr-pro-shell-screens-board | Product shell screens capture source for existing Figma file
- Decision: Added `docs/agent/figma-hypr-pro-shell.html` as the second localhost capture source, containing the actual Hypr-Pro product screens: clean desktop, window chrome, launcher/palette, overview/workspaces, quick settings/notifications, and token-motion-handoff frames. This board is intended for `existingFile` capture into Figma file `3pXzj7Q9suAgilaKd2tfL4`.
- Rationale: The initial bootstrap board establishes audit and token direction, but implementation-ready polish still needs concrete product screens in the same Figma file before node-level code handoff can start.
- Tradeoffs: The second board is still capture-driven HTML, so page separation depends on the Figma import pass rather than native Figma authoring. That preserves speed and implementation fidelity, but future visual tuning inside Figma may still be needed.

- Supersedes: The prior state where the Figma file contained only the bootstrap/audit capture and diagrams, with no concrete product shell screen pack.
- Next validation: Complete the second browser capture, poll to completion, then inspect the resulting Figma file and continue with node-level handoff or refinement.

## 2026-03-12 | gui/hypr-pro-shell-polish-pass | Figma-driven shell chrome migration and rounded panel rendering
- Decision: Updated `src/gfx/velvet_glove.rs` to align the top bar, dock/task strip, launcher, command palette, quick settings, notifications, and workspace overview scenes with the imported Figma Hypr-Pro shell pack. In the same pass, `src/gui/renderer.rs` now honors `corner_radius` for `SolidRect` on both CPU and GPU renderers, so retained shell panels render as rounded products rather than square debug slabs.
- Rationale: The engine contracts were already in place, but the visible shell still looked prototype-grade because the retained scene builders were painting old boxy layouts and the renderer ignored rounded panel geometry even though the render object model already carried radius data.
- Tradeoffs: This pass intentionally prioritizes shell chrome fidelity and interaction hitbox alignment over a full theme-system rewrite. The palette is currently fixed to the Hypr-Pro default tokens inside `hybrid_titan_palette()`, which improves product consistency now but narrows theme variance until the broader theme pipeline catches up.

- Supersedes: The earlier shell state where top bar/task strip/launcher/overlay builders still used the older Hybrid Titan control grammar and where rounded retained rectangles degraded to plain square fills at raster time.
