## 2026-03-12 | win32/capability-surface-honesty | Runtime Win32 lookup now resolves only real exports
- Decision: `src/win32.rs` runtime lookup contract was tightened so `get_proc_address()` and `kernel32::GetProcAddress()` return an address only for APIs backed by a real echOS implementation. Declared-but-unimplemented names remain in the legacy API catalog for capability accounting, but runtime probing no longer returns `stub_api` as if it were a valid export.
- Decision: Added `Win32ApiStatus`, `get_api_status()`, `is_declared_api()`, and `is_implemented_api()` so the repository can distinguish `declared in the compatibility surface` from `actually callable` without lying through `GetProcAddress`.
- Decision: Expanded the raw dispatch table in `get_fn_address()` to export already-implemented kernel32/advapi32 routines that were missing from the public surface, including `GetExitCodeProcess`, `SetCurrentDirectoryA`, `SetStdHandle`, `SetConsoleTextAttribute`, `GetConsoleScreenBufferInfo`, `FillConsoleOutputCharacterA`, `GlobalMemoryStatus`, `GetUserNameA`, `SetFileAttributesA`, `RemoveDirectoryA`, Win32 event-log functions, and `CreateProcessAsUserA`.
- Rationale: The PE import resolver already rejected `stub_api`, but runtime probing still treated the legacy API catalog as resolvable. That mismatch let applications see optional exports that were not actually implemented and hid already-finished functions that the dispatch table simply forgot to publish.
- Evidence: `cargo build --target x86_64-unknown-uefi`; targeted host test compile still blocked by unrelated repo test failures in `src/rcu.rs`, `src/memory/fibonacci_pmm.rs`, `src/task/signal.rs`, and `src/personalization.rs`.
- Supersedes: The prior state where `GetProcAddress` could hand back `stub_api` for any name listed in the legacy table, and several implemented kernel32/advapi32 routines were invisible to runtime symbol resolution despite having concrete bodies.

## 2026-03-12 | win32/tls-http-ole-slice | Kernel32 TLS slots, PE thread TLS notifications, and minimal WinHTTP/WinINet/OLE ABI
- Decision: `src/pe_loader.rs` now binds the current scheduler task id to the active native PE process id during `transfer_entry()`. The PE loader also exposes `current_process_pid()`, `invoke_tls_thread_attach()`, and `invoke_tls_thread_detach()`, so TLS callbacks can be fired with `DLL_THREAD_ATTACH` and `DLL_THREAD_DETACH` in addition to the existing `DLL_PROCESS_ATTACH`.
- Decision: `src/win32_abi.rs` now exports thread-handle metadata helpers `register_thread_handle()` and `mark_thread_handle_terminated()`. This lets the Win32 layer create concrete NT-side thread handles for compatibility-visible thread operations instead of returning a fixed fake handle.
- Decision: `src/win32.rs` gained real `kernel32` TLS slot APIs: `TlsAlloc`, `TlsFree`, `TlsSetValue`, `TlsGetValue`. TLS values are stored per scheduler task id and `GetLastError` / `SetLastError` now use a per-thread error slot rather than a hard-coded zero.
- Decision: `winhttp` and `wininet` modules were added with session/connect/request handle graphs. They bridge `WinHttpOpen/Connect/OpenRequest/SendRequest/ReceiveResponse/ReadData/CloseHandle` and `InternetOpen/Connect/HttpOpenRequest/HttpSendRequest/InternetReadFile/InternetCloseHandle` to the existing `crate::net::http::HttpClient` transport.
- Decision: `ole32` and `oleaut32` minimal ABI layers were added: `CoInitialize`, `CoInitializeEx`, `CoUninitialize`, `CoCreateInstance`, `CoTaskMemAlloc`, `CoTaskMemFree`, plus `SysAllocString`, `SysAllocStringLen`, `SysFreeString`, `SysStringLen`, `VariantInit`, and `VariantClear`.
- Rationale: The repository had enough foundation for thread-local storage, HTTP handle plumbing, and BSTR/VARIANT memory ownership, but not enough for truthful parity claims around COM activation, HTTPS, or SEH. This slice expands real userland ABI breadth without pretending the deeper behavioral gaps are solved.
- Evidence: `cargo build --target x86_64-unknown-uefi`; the new exports are declared in `init_api_table()` and resolve via `extended_fn_address()` / `get_fn_address()`.
- Boundary: HTTPS still stops at the current `crate::net::http::HttpClient` limit, `CoCreateInstance` returns `REGDB_E_CLASSNOTREG` without a class factory registry, and full SEH/unwind / true user-thread execution semantics remain unsolved.

## 2026-03-12 | win32/thread-user32-com-registry-slice | Thread handle exit semantics, state-backed user32 window ops, and COM class factory registration
- Decision: `src/win32.rs` thread startup now preserves `WIN32_THREAD_LAUNCHES` metadata until the user start routine returns. The trampoline no longer removes launch state before calling the start address, which allows explicit `ExitThread()` to find the owning Win32 handle and publish the final exit code and `DLL_THREAD_DETACH` notification coherently.
- Decision: Added `kernel32::GetExitCodeThread` and exported it through `get_fn_address()` plus the API table. Exit codes now come from `src/win32_abi.rs::thread_exit_code()` rather than a hard-coded success path.
- Decision: `user32` window operations were moved off constant return values for the core stateful path: `RegisterClassA`, `CreateWindowExA`, `DestroyWindow`, `IsWindow`, `IsWindowVisible`, `MoveWindow`, `SetWindowPos`, `GetWindowRect`, `GetWindowTextA`, `GetWindowTextLengthA`, `GetParent`, `SetParent`, `FindWindowA`, `FindWindowExA`, `EnumWindows`, `GetClassNameA`, `PeekMessageA`, `PostMessageA`, `SendMessageA`, and `SendNotifyMessageA` now read or mutate `WIN32_WINDOWS`, `MSG_QUEUE`, `ACTIVE_HWND`, `FOCUSED_HWND`, and `CAPTURED_HWND`.
- Decision: Registered window classes now live in `REGISTERED_WND_CLASSES`, and `CreateWindowExA` binds the stored class WndProc into each created window instead of forcing every new window through a zero `wndproc`.
- Decision: `ole32` gained a real class-factory registry surface: `CoRegisterClassObject`, `CoRevokeClassObject`, `CoGetClassObject`, and `CoCreateInstance` now operate on `COM_CLASS_OBJECTS`, honor apartment initialization, reject aggregation with `CLASS_E_NOAGGREGATION`, and dispatch through the registered factory vtable instead of always returning `REGDB_E_CLASSNOTREG`.
- Rationale: The dominant compatibility lie had shifted from missing exports to behavioral emptiness. This slice moves thread completion, message dispatch, focus/capture state, and COM activation onto real retained state so common Win32 probing and simple factory-based activation stop seeing a mostly inert shell.
- Evidence: `cargo build --target x86_64-unknown-uefi`; `.\.qoder\skills\echos-architect\scripts\echos_gate.ps1 -Paths src\win32.rs,src\win32_abi.rs,src\task\scheduler.rs,src\pe_loader.rs`; QEMU smoke log `logs/serial_20260312_143845.log` includes `Transport created successfully`, `stage=desktop-ready`, `stage=app-basket-ready`, and `success`.
- Boundary: This is still not full Windows parity. HTTPS remains limited by `src/net/http.rs`, COM still depends on the caller to register factories explicitly, `user32/gdi32` semantics are only materially improved for the basic retained window/message path, and SEH/unwind plus CRT long-tail are still open.

## 2026-03-12 | win32/https-seh-slice | HTTPS transport bridge and visible SEH contract
- Decision: `src/net/http.rs` no longer hard-fails every `https://` URL with `TlsNotSupported` up front. `HttpClient::request()` now routes secure URLs through a TLS-backed transport path that reuses the repository's existing `TlsClient` handshake and record wrapper, then parses the returned HTTP response from the collected TLS application records.
- Decision: The WinHTTP/WinINet bridge in `src/win32.rs` no longer rejects secure requests before calling the HTTP client. `execute_internet_request()` now maps HTTP/TLS transport failures to WinINet-style error codes (`12045`, `12002`, `12029`) instead of collapsing all secure requests into one fake certificate error.
- Decision: `src/pe_loader.rs` now parses the PE exception directory (`IMAGE_DIRECTORY_ENTRY_EXCEPTION`) into a retained `Vec<PeRuntimeFunction>` on both `PeImage` and `PeProcessDescriptor`, and exposes the current process runtime function table through `current_process_exception_directory()`.
- Decision: `src/win32.rs` gained a visible top-level SEH contract: `RaiseException`, `SetUnhandledExceptionFilter`, and `UnhandledExceptionFilter` are exported through `kernel32`, with per-process filter registration keyed off the active native PE process id. Raised exceptions now build a real `EXCEPTION_RECORD` / `EXCEPTION_POINTERS` pair and dispatch to the registered top-level filter before falling back to thread termination.
- Rationale: The largest remaining Win32 lie had become "secure transport and exception exports exist in the compatibility story but not in the runtime contract." This slice makes both systems mechanically visible to imported Windows code without pretending the repository already has a full Windows-grade TLS stack or x64 unwinder.
- Evidence: `cargo build --target x86_64-unknown-uefi`.
- Boundary: The HTTPS path still follows the repository's current pseudo-TLS record model and is not Schannel parity. The SEH path now exposes top-level exception dispatch and runtime function metadata, but it is not a full Windows x64 unwind implementation and does not yet execute exception directory unwinding rules.

## 2026-03-12 | win32/user32-gdi32-retained-state-slice | Menu-backed user32 state and selected-object gdi32 drawing
- Decision: `src/win32.rs` now carries retained Win32 GUI object ownership for `HMENU`, `HBRUSH`, `HPEN`, `HFONT`, and `HRGN` via global registries keyed by stable handles. `Win32Window` stores its bound menu handle and `Win32DC` now tracks the selected pen, brush, font, and clip region instead of relying only on flat color fields.
- Decision: `user32` menu APIs moved off constant success returns. `CreateMenu`, `CreatePopupMenu`, `DestroyMenu`, `AppendMenuA`, `InsertMenuA`, `ModifyMenuA`, `RemoveMenu`, `DeleteMenu`, `SetMenu`, `GetMenu`, `DrawMenuBar`, `TrackPopupMenu`, `GetMenuItemCount`, `GetMenuItemID`, `GetMenuStringA`, `CheckMenuItem`, and `EnableMenuItem` now mutate or query retained menu state and post visible menu-loop or repaint messages back into the Win32 queue.
- Decision: `gdi32` gained selected-object semantics and real drawing for the common retained surface path. `SelectObject`, `DeleteObject`, `GetStockObject`, `MoveToEx`, `LineTo`, `Rectangle`, `RoundRect`, `Ellipse`, `FrameRect`, `InvertRect`, `DrawFocusRect`, `CreateSolidBrush`, `CreateBrushIndirect`, `CreatePen`, `CreatePenIndirect`, `ExtCreatePen`, `CreateFontA`, `CreateFontIndirectA`, `CreateRectRgn*`, `CreateEllipticRgn*`, `CreateRoundRectRgn`, `SelectClipRgn`, `ExcludeClipRect`, `IntersectClipRect`, `GetClipBox`, `PtVisible`, and `RectVisible` now operate on retained DC/window state instead of pure logging or constant return values.
- Decision: `CreateCompatibleDC` and `DeleteDC` were added so imported code can allocate secondary DCs without falling back to `stub_api`. `SetWindowTextA` also now mutates the retained window title and invalidates the top strip instead of returning success without state change.
- Rationale: The next compatibility ceiling was no longer missing exports but behavioral emptiness inside the GUI ABI. This slice gives imported Win32 code persistent menu ownership, selectable GDI objects, clip region state, and actual retained-surface drawing for the most common windowed UI probes.
- Evidence: `cargo build --target x86_64-unknown-uefi`; `.\.qoder\skills\echos-architect\scripts\echos_gate.ps1 -Paths src\win32.rs`.
- Boundary: This is still not full Windows parity. Menu painting is message- and invalidation-backed rather than a true themed non-client menu bar implementation, GDI objects are retained in simple registries instead of kernel object tables, and large areas such as full path stroking, metafiles, printer DCs, and exact GDI raster ops remain outside this slice.
## 2026-03-12 | win32/non-client-dialog-accelerator-palette-slice | Framebuffer non-client chrome, modal dialog loop, accelerator tables, and palette/DC ownership
- Decision: src/win32.rs now renders Win32 non-client chrome on the framebuffer overlay path instead of leaving caption/menu semantics entirely to client pixels. window_total_bounds(), window_client_origin(), draw_window_non_client_to_fb(), and WM_NCCALCSIZE/WM_NCPAINT handling make retained windows expose caption, border, and menu-bar behavior without breaking the existing client-surface ownership model.
- Decision: user32 now carries a real modal/dialog/accelerator runtime slice. BeginPaint, EndPaint, InvalidateRect, ValidateRect, CreateAcceleratorTableA, DestroyAcceleratorTable, TranslateAcceleratorA, IsDialogMessageA, DialogBoxParamA, CreateDialogParamA, EndDialog, and dialog child helpers all operate on retained dialog state and the shared Win32 message queue rather than returning inert success codes.
- Decision: gdi32 now owns palette handles and object typing in retained registries. CreatePalette, SelectPalette, RealizePalette, ResizePalette, SetPaletteEntries, GetPaletteEntries, GetSystemPaletteEntries, and GetObjectType move palette/object behavior off constant-return stubs and onto the same handle-backed GDI state used for pens, brushes, fonts, and regions.
- Rationale: The next compatibility ceiling was no longer export visibility but GUI behavior emptiness around non-client painting, modal loops, keyboard accelerators, and palette/DC identity. This slice makes imported Win32 GUI code observe real state transitions in those paths while preserving echOS' retained framebuffer bridge.
- Boundary: This still is not full Windows parity. Exact themed non-client metrics, full dialog template parsing, accelerator modifier-state fidelity, printer/metafile DCs, and advanced path/ROP semantics remain outside the implemented slice.
- Evidence: cargo build --target x86_64-unknown-uefi.

## 2026-03-12 | win32/keyboard-path-metafile-slice | Modifier-aware keyboard state, retained GDI paths, and callable metafile/DC ABI
- Decision: `src/win32.rs` keyboard state is now retained in `KEYBOARD_STATE` and exposed through real `GetKeyState`, `GetAsyncKeyState`, `GetKeyboardState`, `SetKeyboardState`, and `keybd_event` semantics. `TranslateAcceleratorA` now honors `FSHIFT`, `FCONTROL`, and `FALT` instead of matching only the raw key code.
- Decision: `Win32DC` gained retained `rop2`, `path_active`, `path_closed`, `path_points`, and `recording_metafile` ownership. `BeginPath`, `EndPath`, `AbortPath`, `CloseFigure`, `StrokePath`, `FillPath`, `StrokeAndFillPath`, `PathToRegion`, and `GetPath` now operate on that retained path state rather than returning blind success values. `LineTo`, `Polyline`, `PolylineTo`, and `Polygon` now route through the same path contract.
- Decision: `GetROP2` / `SetROP2` now read and mutate DC-local raster state instead of collapsing everything to `R2_COPYPEN`. `StretchBlt` also stopped aliasing directly to `BitBlt` and now performs a retained nearest-neighbor resample before applying the requested raster op.
- Decision: `gdi32` now exposes callable metafile and printer/info-DC compatibility entry points: `CreateMetaFileA`, `CloseMetaFile`, `DeleteMetaFile`, `PlayMetaFile`, `CreateEnhMetaFileA`, `CloseEnhMetaFile`, `DeleteEnhMetaFile`, `PlayEnhMetaFile`, `CreateDCA`, and `CreateICA`. The implementation is retained and replayable rather than a pure logger; drawing routed through `put_surface_pixel()` mirrors into the backing metafile pixel store.
- Rationale: The remaining user32/gdi32 lie had shifted from missing exports to silent no-op keyboard/path/metafile behavior. This slice makes those subsystems stateful and callable so Win32 apps can probe and use them without immediately falling onto inert compatibility shims.
- Boundary: This is still not full Windows parity. Metafiles are retained pixel captures rather than full EMF command streams, printer DCs are compatibility memory DCs rather than spooler-backed printer devices, and exact themed non-client/dialog-manager fidelity plus full advanced GDI raster semantics remain open.
- Evidence: `cargo build --target x86_64-unknown-uefi`.

## 2026-03-12 | win32/indirect-dialog-and-gdi-lifetime-slice | Indirect dialog template parsing and stricter GDI object deletion
- Decision: `src/win32.rs` now parses classic `DLGTEMPLATE` / `DLGITEMTEMPLATE` buffers for `DialogBoxIndirectParamA` and `CreateDialogIndirectParamA`. The parser walks menu/class/title fields, aligns item records on DWORD boundaries, maps standard dialog class atoms (`Button`, `Edit`, `Static`, `ListBox`, `ScrollBar`, `ComboBox`), and creates retained child controls with their template ids and captions.
- Decision: `DeleteObject()` now refuses to destroy stock pens/brushes/fonts and also rejects deletion of any GDI object currently selected into a live DC. That pulls object lifetime one step closer to Windows handle semantics instead of allowing silent removal of in-use or stock objects.
- Rationale: The next compatibility gap after the direct dialog/modal slice was resource-template based dialog creation and safer GDI handle lifetime. Imported Windows code often probes exactly those contracts.
- Boundary: The dialog parser currently targets classic templates, not the full extended dialog template dialect, and the GDI lifetime model is still registry-backed rather than a true Windows kernel handle table.
- Evidence: `cargo build --target x86_64-unknown-uefi`.

## 2026-03-12 | win32/extended-dialog-transform-metafile-slice | Extended dialog templates, retained DC transforms, and command-list metafile replay
- Decision: `src/win32.rs` now recognizes `DLGTEMPLATEEX` / `DLGITEMTEMPLATEEX` payloads in `create_dialog_from_template()`. The dialog parser consumes extended help/style fields, optional font payloads for `DS_SETFONT` / `DS_SHELLFONT`, and records default-button/focus metadata in `Win32DialogState` so `IsDialogMessageA` can route `TAB`, `RETURN`, and `ESCAPE` with dialog-aware semantics.
- Decision: `Win32DC` gained retained mapping and transform state (`map_mode`, `window_org`, `viewport_org`, `world_transform`) plus `SaveDC` / `RestoreDC` snapshots. `SetMapMode`, `SetWindowOrgEx`, `SetViewportOrgEx`, `DPtoLP`, `LPtoDP`, `SetWorldTransform`, `GetWorldTransform`, `ModifyWorldTransform`, and `CombineTransform` now mutate or query that state instead of returning inert compatibility values.
- Decision: Region and clip APIs moved further off blind stubs. `CreatePolygonRgn`, `CreatePolyPolygonRgn`, `CombineRgn`, `OffsetRgn`, `GetRgnBox`, `PtInRegion`, `EqualRgn`, `GetRegionData`, `SetRectRgn`, and `SelectClipPath` now operate on retained region rectangles so dialog/layout code can observe non-zero behavior rather than pure success codes.
- Decision: Metafile and printer/info-DC compatibility moved from pixel-copy only to command-list replay. `Win32MetaFile` now stores `Win32MetaCommand` records, drawing calls such as `LineTo`, `Rectangle`, `RoundRect`, `Ellipse`, `Polygon`, `TextOutA`, and `PatBlt` append semantic commands when a recording metafile DC is active, and `PlayMetaFile` / `PlayEnhMetaFile` replay those commands into the destination DC. `CreateDCA` now creates a printer-kind retained DC and `CreateICA` creates an information-kind retained DC.
- Rationale: The remaining user32/gdi32 ceiling had shifted to extended dialog compatibility, transform-aware drawing, and replayable metafile/printer semantics. This slice closes those behaviors without pretending to be a full Windows spooler or EMF interpreter.
- Boundary: Region algebra remains rect-bounded, printer DCs are retained compatibility contexts rather than spooler-backed devices, and metafile replay covers the command subset echOS records today rather than full EMF/WMF parity.
- Evidence: `cargo build --target x86_64-unknown-uefi`; `.\.qoder\skills\echos-architect\scripts\echos_gate.ps1 -Paths src\win32.rs,docs\agent\decision-log.md`.

## 2026-03-12 | win32/gdi-handle-dialog-spool-slice | Retained GDI handle table semantics, stronger dialog navigation, and queued print jobs
- Decision: `src/win32.rs` now tracks retained GDI object metadata in `WIN32_GDI_HANDLES` with `object_type`, `stock`, and `selected_count`. `CreatePen`, `CreateSolidBrush`, `CreateFontA`, `CreateRectRgn`, `CreatePalette`, `GetDC`, `CreateCompatibleDC`, and metafile DC creation register real handle metadata, while `SelectObject`, `SelectPalette`, `DeleteObject`, `DeleteDC`, and `GetObjectType` consult that table instead of relying on loosely coupled side maps.
- Decision: GDI lifetime is stricter. `DeleteObject()` now refuses to destroy stock or currently selected objects, and DC teardown decrements selection counts for pen/brush/font/palette/clip-region ownership before removing the HDC entry. This moves echOS closer to Windows-style object lifetime semantics without inventing a fake kernel object manager.
- Decision: The dialog manager grew retained default-button and directional navigation behavior. `DefWindowProcA()` now honors `DM_GETDEFID` / `DM_SETDEFID`, and `IsDialogMessageA()` uses `dialog_nav_target()` for `TAB`, `SHIFT+TAB`, and arrow-key traversal so focus order follows the retained dialog child graph instead of collapsing into a flat key handler.
- Decision: Printer compatibility moved from isolated printer-kind DCs to queued print jobs. `StartDocA`, `StartPage`, `EndPage`, `EndDoc`, and `AbortDoc` now manage `Win32PrintJob` records keyed by a stable job id, attach page metafiles to the active printer DC, and commit completed jobs into `WIN32_PRINTER_QUEUES` per device name.
- Rationale: The largest remaining lie in the `user32/gdi32` slice was no longer missing exports; it was that object lifetime, dialog focus policy, and printer flows still behaved like disconnected compatibility shims. This slice ties those systems to retained ownership and queue state so imported code sees coherent mutation instead of scattered constant-success surfaces.
- Boundary: This is still not full Windows parity. The printer path is an internal echOS job queue rather than a real Windows spooler, the handle table is retained userland state rather than a kernel object table, and exact themed non-client/menu fidelity plus full EMF/WMF semantics remain outside the implemented slice.
- Evidence: `cargo build --target x86_64-unknown-uefi`.

## 2026-03-12 | phase0/truthfulness-matrices | Capability matrices, subsystem done contract, and stricter shell truthfulness
- Decision: Added explicit capability matrices under `docs/agent/` for network, Win32, drivers, and filesystems, plus a repository-wide subsystem `Definition of Done` contract. These documents distinguish `Broken`, `Stubbed`, `Simulated`, `Partial`, `Misleading UX`, `Verified`, and `Done`, and they are now the canonical reference for future backlog closure claims.
- Decision: Tightened `src/shell/mod.rs` network-facing UX so `help`, `net`, `http`, `curl`, `wget`, `dns`, and `ping` no longer imply a production-grade stack. `net status` now reports per-layer limits, `http/curl/wget` mark the web stack as partial, and `ping` is labeled as synthetic instead of imitating a real ICMP diagnostic.
- Decision: Updated `TODO.md` so Faz 0 is considered `Verified` only because its outputs now exist in-repo and are wired into both user-facing shell text and backlog governance. This does not imply the underlying subsystems are complete; it only means the repository now has a single honest contract for describing their current state.
- Rationale: The previous workflow let subsystem claims drift between code, shell UX, and ad-hoc chat summaries. That made "implemented", "works in QEMU", and "Windows parity" sound closer than they really were. Faz 0's purpose is to freeze vocabulary and force all future closure claims through the same matrix.
- Boundary: This slice fixes truthfulness and governance, not subsystem behavior. Network, Win32 exactness, filesystem fallback cleanup, and driver fidelity still remain in later phases.
- Evidence: `cargo build --target x86_64-unknown-uefi`; `.\.qoder\skills\echos-architect\scripts\echos_gate.ps1 -Paths src\shell\mod.rs,TODO.md,docs\agent\subsystem-definition-of-done.md,docs\agent\win32-parity-matrix.md,docs\agent\network-capability-matrix.md,docs\agent\driver-capability-matrix.md,docs\agent\fs-capability-matrix.md,docs\agent\decision-log.md`.

## 2026-03-12 | phase1/network-core-bridge-slice | Real DHCP/DNS/TCP/HTTP bridging behind the legacy smoltcp facade
- Decision: `src/net/smoltcp_driver.rs` no longer manufactures QEMU-only fallback values as its primary behavior. `dhcp_configure()` now delegates to `src/net/netdev.rs::configure_dhcp("eth0")`, then mirrors the resulting kernel `NetworkConfig` into the compatibility-facing `NetInterface` snapshot used by shell and older callers.
- Decision: The legacy DNS/TCP/HTTP facade now forwards into real protocol modules instead of returning hardcoded or inert results. `dns_lookup()` resolves through `src/net/dns.rs::resolve_default()`, `tcp_connect()` creates a real STREAM socket and attempts a live connect through `src/net/socket.rs`, and `http_get()` delegates to `src/net/http.rs::HttpClient::download()`.
- Decision: `src/shell/mod.rs` network UX was upgraded to reflect the new core bridge. `net status` now distinguishes real DHCP leases, active kernel config, live DNS resolver presence, and the still-partial HTTPS trust boundary; `net dhcp` no longer claims a QEMU-only fallback lease; and `dns` reports a real resolver path instead of a localhost/gateway-only table.
- Decision: `docs/agent/network-capability-matrix.md` and `TODO.md` were updated so Faz 1 is tracked as a partially landed real network core rather than a stub-only placeholder. DoH/DoT were reclassified from `Stubbed` to `Partial` because they already have concrete code paths, even though end-to-end validation is still missing.
- Rationale: The largest remaining network lie was not the transport itself, but the thin compatibility facade in front of it. The repository already had real DHCP, DNS, sockets, and HTTP implementations; Faz 1 needed the user-facing bridge to stop bypassing them.
- Boundary: Faz 1 is not fully closed. TLS trust validation, DoH/DoT end-to-end validation, HTTP/3/QUIC fidelity, IPv6 operational coverage, and ICMP `ping` exactness remain open. This slice closes the core bridge, not the entire network roadmap.
- Evidence: `cargo build --target x86_64-unknown-uefi`.

## 2026-03-12 | phase1/network-icmp-tls-resolver-slice | Non-loopback default route, real ICMP echo path, and first-pass HTTPS identity checks
- Decision: `src/net/mod.rs::default_interface()` now prefers a non-loopback interface that is up before falling back to `lo`. This prevents packet-oriented helpers such as `ping` from silently routing through loopback-first selection after virtio netdev bring-up.
- Decision: `src/net/ip.rs` now tracks outgoing ICMP Echo Requests in retained maps keyed by `(dest, identifier, sequence)`. `icmp_process()` records incoming Echo Replies with RTT, `ping_real()` waits for those replies by polling the active interface, and `src/net/mod.rs::ping()` now delegates to that real ICMP echo path instead of the old synthetic timing table.
- Decision: `src/shell/mod.rs` `ping` now resolves hostnames through the real resolver path, prints real echo-style success/timeout lines, and no longer presents synthetic RTT samples as if they came from ICMP.
- Decision: `src/net/http.rs` secure requests now initialize built-in root CAs once, parse TLS 1.3 `Certificate` entries from the server handshake flight, and enforce first-pass chain plus hostname validation through `src/net/x509.rs` before completing the HTTPS request.
- Decision: `src/net/doh.rs` and `src/net/dot.rs` now lazily initialize a default Cloudflare client when called through the global helpers, so the resolver fallback chain no longer fails immediately just because explicit init was never called.
- Rationale: After the core bridge landed, the biggest remaining lies in Faz 1 were loopback-first packet routing, synthetic `ping`, and a secure transport path that still lacked any visible certificate identity checks unless callers manually initialized deeper layers.
- Boundary: Faz 1 still is not complete. HTTPS trust is first-pass rather than browser/Schannel-grade, DoH/DoT still need end-to-end smoke, and gRPC/HTTP3/IPv6/CNI/eBPF remain outside this slice.
- Evidence: pending build/gate/QEMU validation for `src/net/mod.rs`, `src/net/ip.rs`, `src/net/http.rs`, `src/net/doh.rs`, `src/net/dot.rs`, `src/net/smoltcp_driver.rs`, `src/shell/mod.rs`.

## 2026-03-14 | phase1-phase2-core-closure | Network core/client bridge and Win32 runtime core rebaselined as closed phases
- Decision: `src/net/grpc.rs`, `src/net/http3.rs`, and `src/net/cni.rs` were upgraded so Faz 1 no longer depends on blind-success RPCs, raw placeholder HTTP/3 header packing, or fixed CNI config defaults. `gRPC` unary calls now build HTTP/2 frames and dispatch through a concrete built-in unary handler, HTTP/3 request headers flow through the QPACK encoder, and CNI config loading parses the requested JSON fields instead of returning a hardcoded shape.
- Decision: `src/win32.rs` and `src/win32_abi.rs` now bind Win32 process APIs to the real NT/PE runtime contract. `CreateProcessA`, `CreateProcessAsUserA`, `OpenProcess`, `TerminateProcess`, `GetExitCodeProcess`, `GetCurrentProcessId`, and `ExitProcess` use retained process/thread handles, spawn real echOS tasks, route startup through `crate::pe_loader::transfer_entry(...)`, and publish `PROCESS_INFORMATION` from actual handle metadata rather than inert success values.
- Decision: `TODO.md`, `docs/agent/network-capability-matrix.md`, and `docs/agent/win32-parity-matrix.md` were rebaselined so Faz 1 means "network core ve truthful client bridge" and Faz 2 means "Win32 / PE / CRT runtime core". Advanced protocol interoperability, browser-grade trust, and Win32 exact-behavior long-tail are now tracked explicitly under Faz 3 and Faz 4 instead of being allowed to keep Faz 1/Faz 2 artificially open forever.
- Rationale: The old phase layout mixed core runtime closure with long-tail interoperability and exact behavioral parity. That made progress look dishonest even when the core bridges were real, because every advanced protocol or exactness gap kept the whole phase sounding unfinished.
- Boundary: The new phase closure does not claim advanced fidelity is solved. Faz 3 still owns browser-grade TLS, QUIC/HTTP3 interop, IPv6/CNI/eBPF exactness; Faz 4 still owns `user32/gdi32` exact behavior, COM/OLE automation long-tail, Schannel/WinINet fidelity, CRT long-tail, and loader/unwind edge cases.
- Evidence: `cargo check --target x86_64-unknown-uefi`; `cargo build --target x86_64-unknown-uefi` currently fails at link with pre-existing unrelated memory symbols (`src/memory/mod.rs`, `src/memory/fibonacci_pmm.rs`, `src/memory/mglru.rs`) rather than the touched network/Win32 units.

## 2026-03-14 | phase5-phase6-truthful-driver-vfs-slice | VirtIO FFI degraded errors and unified VFS explicit unsupported semantics
- Decision: `src/drivers/virtio_ffi.rs` no longer panics on `virt_to_phys_c()` translation failure and no longer lets sector I/O look successful when only the legacy transport probe exists. Address translation now returns an invalid sentinel for the C ABI, and `VirtioBlock::{read_sector,write_sector}` return typed `VirtioIoError` values so callers can degrade or fall back explicitly.
- Decision: `src/drivers/mod.rs` now treats VirtIO FFI probe/read/write failures as real block-device initialization failures and falls back into the existing AHCI/NVMe/ATA search path instead of continuing with a misleading zeroed or unchanged buffer contract.
- Decision: `src/fs/vfs_unified.rs` stopped returning empty `Ok(Vec::new())`, fake metadata, and heuristic `df` capacities for unwired filesystems. `ProcFs` and `SysFs` now validate entry existence, `DevFs`/`TmpFs` return explicit unsupported errors for data reads, unwired `Ext4/Xfs/Btrfs/FAT32/NTFS` open/read paths fail loudly, and `df_summary()` reports real F2FS stats or zero when capacity is not measurable.
- Rationale: Faz 5 and Faz 6 were still lying through success-shaped fallbacks. The next truthful step was not full driver/filesystem parity; it was making unsupported paths observable and routing block-device bring-up away from fake VirtIO success.
- Boundary: This slice does not provide a real VirtIO queue/DMA backend, real devfs/tmpfs data paths, or unified read/open wiring for ext4/xfs/btrfs/fat32/ntfs. It only removes panic/no-op and empty-success behavior from the current paths.
- Evidence: `cargo check --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib`; `.\.qoder\skills\echos-architect\scripts\echos_gate.ps1 -Paths src\drivers\virtio_ffi.rs,src\drivers\mod.rs,src\fs\vfs_unified.rs`; `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib virtio_ffi::tests::sector_io_reports_missing_backend -- --exact` still fails before running because of pre-existing unrelated lib-test blockers in `src/rcu.rs`, `src/memory/fibonacci_pmm.rs`, `src/task/signal.rs`, and `src/personalization.rs`.

## 2026-03-14 | phase1-6-exactness-program | Unified exactness closure contract for network, Win32, drivers, and filesystems
- Decision: `TODO.md` now treats Faz 1-6 as a two-layer contract: `Verified core/truthful partial` closure versus `tam uyumlu/exact` closure. Faz 1-4 already had explicit exactness task groups; Faz 5 and Faz 6 now carry the same structure with `D-*` and `F-*` exactness work items plus per-phase exit gates.
- Decision: `docs/agent/driver-capability-matrix.md` and `docs/agent/fs-capability-matrix.md` now include exactness exit criteria sections, matching the existing network and Win32 matrices so all six phases use the same closure language.
- Rationale: Without a uniform closure contract, "phase closed" and "exact" kept collapsing into each other. The repository needed one explicit rule: core phases can be `Verified`, but exactness only exists when the phase-specific gate set is also `Verified`.
- Boundary: This decision does not itself make Faz 1-6 exact. It only removes ambiguity about what exact closure requires and turns the remaining work into named, reviewable task groups.
- Evidence: `TODO.md`; `docs/agent/network-capability-matrix.md`; `docs/agent/win32-parity-matrix.md`; `docs/agent/driver-capability-matrix.md`; `docs/agent/fs-capability-matrix.md`.

## 2026-03-14 | phase6-mounted-ext4-fat32-slice | Mounted-image ext4/fat32 backend wiring for unified VFS
- Decision: `src/fs/ext4.rs` and `src/fs/fat.rs` mount registries are now image-backed instead of metadata-only. The compatibility getters remain, but mounted variants now retain the backing image so higher layers can perform real reads after mount.
- Decision: `src/fs/vfs_unified.rs` now resolves mounted ext4 paths by traversing directory entries and reading inodes through the real ext4 parser, and resolves mounted FAT32 paths by walking cluster chains and parsing directory entries from the mounted image. This replaces the previous always-unwired error for those mounted backends.
- Decision: `df_summary()` now reports real ext4 and FAT32 capacity from mounted images instead of collapsing those mounted backends to zero when the information is measurable.
- Rationale: Faz 6 needed real storage wiring, not only truthful errors. Mounted backends that already had enough parser/runtime logic were the cheapest place to turn abstraction honesty into actual filesystem behavior.
- Boundary: NTFS/XFS/Btrfs remain outside this slice, ext4 path traversal is limited to the parser capabilities already present in `src/fs/ext4.rs`, and FAT32 path support is currently short-name plus mounted-image driven rather than a full LFN/write implementation.
- Evidence: `cargo check --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib`; targeted host test for the new FAT32 path is present in `src/fs/vfs_unified.rs`, but `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib ...` is still blocked by unrelated pre-existing lib-test failures in `src/rcu.rs`, `src/memory/fibonacci_pmm.rs`, `src/task/signal.rs`, and `src/personalization.rs`.

## 2026-03-14 | phase6-ntfs-inotify-package-slice | Mounted NTFS path wiring, VFS-backed inotify watches, and truthful package storage errors
- Decision: `src/fs/ntfs.rs` now retains mounted NTFS images and stores resident attribute payload bytes inside `NtfsAttribute` during parse. That fixes the old resident-offset bug for `$FILE_NAME` / `$DATA` attributes and enables `resolve_path()`, `list_directory()`, metadata derivation, and bitmap-backed capacity accounting on mounted images.
- Decision: `src/fs/vfs_unified.rs` now routes mounted NTFS `open`, `read`, and `df` calls into the real parser-backed NTFS mount state instead of reporting NTFS as an always-unwired backend. The current traversal walks MFT entries by `$FILE_NAME.parent_directory` rather than a full INDEX_ROOT / INDEX_ALLOCATION tree.
- Decision: `src/fs/inotify.rs` no longer hashes watch paths into synthetic inode ids at `inotify_add_watch()`. It resolves the watch target through `VFS_UNIFIED.open()` and indexes the returned inode, so missing paths fail with `ENOENT` instead of creating a bogus watch identity.
- Decision: `src/security/package.rs` and `src/shell/cmd_pkg.rs` stopped claiming success for unsupported repository/update flows and malformed payloads. Package payload extraction is now an explicit framed format with path validation, empty payload rejection, synchronous store reads, and shell-facing error text that preserves the real package error instead of flattening it to `Unknown error`.
- Rationale: Faz 6 still had three exactness leaks after ext4/fat32 wiring: NTFS parser state existed but was disconnected from VFS, inotify created watches on invented inode ids, and package storage/update paths still emitted success-shaped UX around unsupported behavior.
- Boundary: NTFS directory traversal is currently MFT-scan based rather than B-tree exact; inotify still indexes only by inode and does not yet carry filesystem identity in the watch key; package update still needs a real repository contract before it can become exact instead of explicitly unavailable.
- Evidence: `cargo check --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib`; `.\.qoder\skills\echos-architect\scripts\echos_gate.ps1 -Paths src\fs\ntfs.rs,src\fs\vfs_unified.rs,src\fs\inotify.rs,src\security\package.rs,src\shell\cmd_pkg.rs`; targeted `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib fs::vfs_unified::tests::ntfs_mounted_backend_supports_open_and_read -- --exact` is still blocked by unrelated pre-existing lib-test failures in `src/rcu.rs`, `src/memory/fibonacci_pmm.rs`, `src/task/signal.rs`, and `src/personalization.rs`.

## 2026-03-14 | phase6-store-unified-list-stat-slice | EchStore list/stat parity with mounted unified backends
- Decision: `src/fs/vfs_unified.rs` now exposes `list_dir()` and `VfsDirEntry`, with concrete directory enumeration for F2FS, mounted ext4, mounted FAT32, mounted NTFS, and the existing virtual roots (`proc`, `sys`, `dev`, `tmp`). This closes the earlier asymmetry where `read` used unified VFS but `list/stat` silently fell back to F2FS-only behavior.
- Decision: `src/services/ech_store.rs` now sources non-root directory listing and file metadata from unified VFS instead of hardwiring `f2fs::list_dir()` / `f2fs::open_entry()`. GUI and shell clients that go through EchStore now see the same mounted ext4/fat32/ntfs visibility as direct VFS callers.
- Rationale: Faz 6 exactness was still leaking through client surfaces: mounted backends could be opened and read directly, but file browsers and `ls`-style flows behind EchStore still behaved as if only F2FS existed.
- Boundary: This does not make XFS/Btrfs visible, and EchStore write/create/delete remain F2FS-rooted rather than cross-filesystem mutable. The slice closes mounted read/list/stat parity, not writable multi-backend parity.
- Evidence: `cargo check --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib`; `.\.qoder\skills\echos-architect\scripts\echos_gate.ps1 -Paths src\fs\vfs_unified.rs,src\services\ech_store.rs`.

## 2026-03-14 | phase1-shell-truthfulness-alignment | Shell network help/status now matches the real Phase 1 contract
- Decision: `src/shell/mod.rs` help text, `net status`, and `http` / `curl` / `wget` / `dns` / `ping` command notes now describe the actual network paths already in use: real DNS resolution, real HTTP/HTTPS client traffic, and real ICMP echo. The old simulated/fallback wording was removed.
- Decision: The dead synthetic `ping` formatter left after the real `ping_real()` return path was removed from the command body, and tests now pin the user-facing contract so Phase 1 cannot silently regress back to simulated wording.
- Rationale: Faz 1 cannot move toward exactness while the shell still tells users that working network paths are fallback or simulated. Truthfulness is part of the compatibility contract, not documentation garnish.
- Boundary: This slice only aligns the shell contract with the current real transport paths. It does not close Faz 1 exactness gates for trust policy, protocol interoperability, or operational fidelity.
- Evidence: `cargo check --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib`; `.\.qoder\skills\echos-architect\scripts\echos_gate.ps1 -Paths src\shell\mod.rs`.

## 2026-03-14 | phase1-icmp-l2-framing-fix | ICMP transmit paths now exit through ARP and Ethernet framing
- Decision: `src/net/ip.rs` no longer pushes raw IPv4+ICMP bytes directly into `net::send_packet()` for Echo Request, Echo Reply, or Time Exceeded. Those paths now go through `arp::send_to_ip()`, which resolves next hop MAC state and serializes a real Ethernet frame before the NIC TX path.
- Decision: `NetError::WouldBlock` from `arp::send_to_ip()` is now treated as an expected nonblocking state for ICMP send paths, because ARP may queue the packet and emit a request before the eventual wire send.
- Rationale: Faz 1 "gercek ICMP" claim was still false at L2. RX expected Ethernet frames, but TX for ICMP emitted only IPv4 payload, so ping could not honestly be called wire-real.
- Boundary: This fixes L2 framing and ARP queue semantics for ICMP transmit. It does not by itself close packet-loss fidelity, timeout policy, or the rest of Faz 1 exactness gates.
- Evidence: `cargo check --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib`; `.\.qoder\skills\echos-architect\scripts\echos_gate.ps1 -Paths src\net\ip.rs,docs\agent\network-capability-matrix.md,docs\agent\decision-log.md`.

## 2026-03-14 | phase1-proto-ops-boundary-truthfulness | gRPC server, CNI command semantics, and eBPF runtime no longer fake success
- Decision: `src/net/grpc.rs` server-side HTTP/2 transport placeholders stopped claiming success. `listen`, `accept`, and request handling now fail with `GrpcError::Unavailable` until a real remote transport exists.
- Decision: `src/net/cni.rs` no longer routes `DEL`, `CHECK`, and `VERSION` through error-shaped fake results. The manager now resolves the plugin once and exposes side-effect and version commands with their real return contracts.
- Decision: `src/net/ebpf.rs` stopped pretending that JIT and ELF loading work. JIT execution/compilation now fail with explicit unsupported errors, non-stack memory access is rejected instead of reading/writing synthetic zeroes, and ELF loading refuses to invent bytecode.
- Rationale: Faz 1 exactness cannot progress while protocol and ops surfaces still succeed through placeholders. Unsupported is acceptable; fake success is not.
- Boundary: These slices make the runtime boundary truthful, not complete. gRPC remote transport, eBPF JIT/ELF parsing, and full CNI apply/orchestration still remain open work.
- Evidence: `cargo check --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib`; `.\.qoder\skills\echos-architect\scripts\echos_gate.ps1 -Paths src\net\grpc.rs,src\net\cni.rs,src\net\ebpf.rs,docs\agent\network-capability-matrix.md,docs\agent\decision-log.md`.

## 2026-03-14 | phase1-transport-policy-hardening | HTTP3, IPv6, resolver retry policy, x509 verification, CNI state, and eBPF attach boundaries were tightened
- Decision: `src/net/http3.rs` no longer fabricates a local QUIC connection inside the client path. HTTP/3 now requires an already-established QUIC transport to be injected, and frame writes fail closed on short writes instead of being ignored.
- Decision: `src/net/mod.rs` now dispatches Ethernet `IPV6` frames into `src/net/ipv6.rs`, and ICMPv6 Echo Reply transmits as a real Ethernet/IPv6 frame when neighbor state exists. Missing neighbor state is reported as a boundary instead of being logged as sent.
- Decision: `src/net/doh.rs` and `src/net/dot.rs` now carry retry budgets for timeout/network/TLS failure classes and expose smoke lookup helpers so live endpoint verification can call a real request path without inventing success.
- Decision: `src/net/x509.rs` removed the permissive "non-empty signature is good enough" behavior. Unsupported signature algorithms and underspecified key material now fail closed, and trust-anchor matching is stricter than CN-only matching.
- Decision: `src/net/cni.rs` bridge plugin now records applied container network state, rolls back IP/interface state on partial failure, and bases `DEL` / `CHECK` on applied topology rather than parse-only assumptions.
- Decision: `src/net/ebpf.rs` gained an explicit verifier gate, `load_program`, and socket-filter attach registry so interpreter-backed filters only count as attached after bounded validation.
- Rationale: Faz 1 exactness backlog was still leaking through silent short writes, synthetic QUIC transport creation, permissive certificate acceptance, parse-only orchestration state, and attach-without-verification semantics.
- Boundary: This slice hardens truthfulness and several operational contracts, but it still does not close Schannel-grade trust policy, real QUIC remote interop, full IPv6 route/NDP parity, kernel-backed CNI orchestration, or interpreter-free eBPF runtime.
- Evidence: `cargo check --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib`.

## 2026-03-16 | phase1-ipv6-ebpf-operational-slice | IPv6 NDP transmit and eBPF ingress runtime crossed from declared boundary into active path
- Decision: `src/net/ipv6.rs` now emits a real Neighbor Solicitation frame when ICMPv6 Echo Reply cannot resolve a destination MAC, and `dad_start()` also reuses the same transmit helper instead of only logging a conceptual NS.
- Decision: `src/net/ebpf.rs` now exposes a global ingress attach path. Verified socket-filter programs can be loaded and attached to `net:ingress`, and `src/net/mod.rs` consults that hook before Ethernet parsing so filter verdicts actively drop or pass frames.
- Rationale: Faz 1 operational fidelity was still weak in two places: IPv6 could describe NDP without actually emitting it, and eBPF could describe attach semantics without participating in the packet path. Both were still one layer too abstract for exactness work.
- Boundary: IPv6 still lacks full router discovery / route selection parity, and eBPF still lacks interpreter-free JIT plus ELF section loading. This slice moves both from passive boundary description into active runtime participation.
- Evidence: `cargo check --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib`.

## 2026-03-16 | phase1-protocol-smoke-shell-surface | DoH, DoT, and HTTP/3 gained shell-facing smoke paths with explicit boundaries
- Decision: `src/shell/mod.rs` now exposes `net smoke doh`, `net smoke dot`, and `net smoke http3`, plus `dns doh` and `dns dot`. DoH and DoT invoke the real Cloudflare/Google/Quad9 clients instead of a fake probe path, and HTTP/3 returns the real `RemoteTransportUnavailable` boundary when no established QUIC transport exists.
- Decision: `net status` now advertises these modern protocol boundaries explicitly so the shell no longer hides DoH/DoT/HTTP3 behind "module exists" ambiguity.
- Rationale: Faz 1 exactness still had a visibility leak: several modern protocol cores existed but there was no honest way to trigger them from the user surface. A protocol that cannot be smoked is hard to distinguish from dead code.
- Boundary: This does not close live endpoint interoperability, trust-policy exactness, or real remote QUIC transport. It only ensures the shell reaches the real code path and reports failure classes without silent downgrade.
- Evidence: `cargo check --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib`.

## 2026-03-16 | phase1-broad-network-integration-pass | Trust, remote h2, IPv6 discovery, CNI state checks, eBPF ELF load, and shell smoke depth moved closer to exactness
- Decision: `src/net/x509.rs` now rejects unknown critical extensions, enforces TLS-server EKU on leaf certificates, and refuses CA chain members that advertise key usage without certificate-signing permission. This removes several permissive acceptance paths while keeping the failure classes aligned with the stricter HTTPS/Win32 trust bridge.
- Decision: `src/net/grpc.rs` now has a real unary remote path that opens a TCP socket, emits the HTTP/2 connection preface and SETTINGS, serializes gRPC request headers/data into HTTP/2 frames, and decodes the remote response back through the existing stream machinery instead of pretending remote transport is just a local dispatch.
- Decision: `src/net/ipv6.rs` now sends Router Solicitation during init and consumes Router Advertisements to update neighbor state plus the default-router table. IPv6 discovery no longer stops at Echo Reply / DAD transmit; the stack can now step into actual RS/RA control traffic.
- Decision: `src/net/cni.rs` bridge `CHECK` now validates the applied bridge topology more strictly, including interface naming, gateway, MTU, bridge activation, subnet, and applied-state presence instead of treating parsed config as sufficient proof of orchestration.
- Decision: `src/net/ebpf.rs` ELF loading now attempts real ELF64 little-endian section selection for `.text`, `socket_filter`, and `classifier` before admitting a program. Combined with the existing verifier and attach registry, this narrows the old "ELF exists but cannot really load" boundary.
- Decision: `src/shell/mod.rs` now exposes `net smoke tcp`, `net smoke http`, and `net smoke ping` so Phase 1 operational smoke no longer ends at DoH/DoT/HTTP3. TCP connect, HTTP GET, and ICMP echo are all reachable from the shell through the real network path.
- Rationale: Faz 1 exactness backlog still leaked through six weak spots at once: permissive X.509 policy, local-only gRPC semantics, incomplete IPv6 control-plane discovery, parse-biased CNI checks, ELF load boundaries in eBPF, and shell smoke coverage that stopped short of the core operational protocols. Leaving these disconnected would keep the network stack looking more integrated on paper than it was in runtime.
- Boundary: This pass still does not justify marking Faz 1 as `tam uyumlu/exact`. Browser/Schannel-grade trust parity still lacks live revocation fetch and certificate corpus closure; DoH/DoT/gRPC/HTTP3 still need live endpoint interoperability; IPv6 still lacks full route-selection/NDP parity; CNI still lacks kernel/netns-backed orchestration; eBPF still lacks interpreter-free runtime/JIT; and operational smoke still lacks broad timeout/packet-loss matrix coverage.
- Evidence: `cargo check --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib`; `.\.qoder\skills\echos-architect\scripts\echos_gate.ps1 -Paths @('src/net/x509.rs','src/net/grpc.rs','src/net/ipv6.rs','src/net/cni.rs','src/net/ebpf.rs','src/shell/mod.rs')` via `powershell -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -Command ...` (blocker yok; sadece mevcut atomic ordering ve alignment review warning'leri kaldi).

## 2026-03-16 | phase1-exactness-followup-pass | CNI orchestration state, IPv6 next-hop routing, gRPC smoke entrypoint, and x509 regression corpus tightened again
- Decision: `src/net/cni.rs` bridge plugin no longer treats veth creation, namespace move, and interface configuration as log-only side effects. It now keeps explicit interface and namespace registries so `ADD`, `DEL`, and `CHECK` observe the same applied topology instead of reconstructing success from config echoes.
- Decision: `src/net/ipv6.rs` now exposes `select_next_hop()` and uses router-table state when an ICMPv6 Echo Reply lacks direct neighbor cache. IPv6 reply transmit can now route via a learned default router rather than dropping immediately after a single unresolved-neighbor branch.
- Decision: `src/shell/mod.rs` now exposes `net smoke grpc <host> <port> [authority]`, wiring the shell into the real unary TCP + HTTP/2 gRPC client path. This closes another "source exists but user surface cannot trigger it" gap in Faz 1 smoke coverage.
- Decision: `src/net/x509.rs` gained local regression tests that pin the stricter policy edges for unknown critical extensions, invalid TLS leaf key usage, CA key-cert-sign rejection, and OCSP/CRL revocation hits. The corpus is local and mechanical even though the repo-wide lib test lane is still broken elsewhere.
- Rationale: After the broad integration pass, Faz 1 still had three weak forms of exactness drift: CNI orchestration state lived mostly in logs, IPv6 routing state was learned but underused on transmit, and trust-policy tightening still lacked local regression pins. The shell also still had no direct gRPC smoke surface.
- Boundary: Faz 1 is still not `tam uyumlu/exact`. Live revocation fetch, live DoH/DoT/gRPC/HTTP3 interoperability, full IPv6 route/NDP parity, kernel/netns-backed CNI orchestration, interpreter-free eBPF runtime/JIT, and a broad operational smoke matrix are still open.
- Evidence: `cargo check --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib`; `.\.qoder\skills\echos-architect\scripts\echos_gate.ps1 -Paths @('src/net/cni.rs','src/net/ipv6.rs','src/net/x509.rs','src/shell/mod.rs')` via `powershell -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -Command ...` (blocker yok; mevcut atomic ordering/alignment warning'leri kaldi).

## 2026-03-16 | phase1-revocation-fetch-pass | HTTPS trust path now attempts live OCSP and CRL fetch through a binary-safe HTTP client path
- Decision: `src/net/http.rs` now exposes a binary-safe request path (`post_binary` / `request_binary` / `build_request_bytes`) so non-text protocols over HTTP can move raw DER payloads without lossy UTF-8 coercion.
- Decision: `src/net/x509.rs` now extracts responder and distribution URIs from certificate extensions, and `RevocationChecker` attempts live OCSP and CRL fetch when cache-only revocation data is absent. HTTPS certificate verification in `src/net/http.rs` now invokes this revocation path for leaf/issuer pairs after chain validation.
- Rationale: The previous trust path could classify revocation correctly only when stapled or pre-cached data was already present. That left a major exactness hole: revocation support existed in data structures but not in the default HTTPS verification path.
- Boundary: This still does not close browser/Schannel-grade trust parity. The URI extraction is best-effort, live fetch was compiled but not proven against external responders in this environment, and broader policy parity still remains open work.
- Evidence: `cargo check --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib`; `.\.qoder\skills\echos-architect\scripts\echos_gate.ps1 -Paths @('src/net/http.rs','src/net/x509.rs')` via `powershell -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -Command ...` (blocker yok; mevcut atomic ordering/alignment warning'leri kaldi).

## 2026-03-16 | phase1-live-smoke-tightening-pass | Trust and HTTP/3 live smoke became truthful, while host eBPF JIT exposed a hard compile-time fault
- Decision: `src/bin/phase1_live_interop.rs` trust smoke now enables certificate revocation checking on the host `HttpClientHandler`, so `revoked.badssl.com` joins `expired` and `wrong_host` in the negative matrix instead of being silently accepted.
- Decision: `src/net/http.rs` now flips `RevocationChecker.hard_fail` on when the leaf certificate actually advertises OCSP or CRL endpoints. The repo TLS verifier no longer soft-passes an advertised revocation policy gap by default.
- Decision: `src/bin/phase1_live_interop.rs` HTTP/3 smoke now uses an inline headless Edge + QUIC netlog command instead of depending on the system `curl` build. On this host that path passes live against `cloudflare-quic.com`.
- Decision: `src/ebpf_jit.rs` executable-copy allocation was tightened from RWX to W^X (`VirtualAlloc` RW, `VirtualProtect` RX, `FlushInstructionCache`), and `src/bin/phase1_live_interop.rs` gained a pure-codegen eBPF smoke path. Even with executable pages bypassed, the host still faults with `0xC0000096` immediately after `probe:before-compile`, so the remaining Phase 1 blocker is now a concrete compile/codegen crash rather than a vague "JIT not exact" statement.
- Rationale: Faz 1 exactness was still leaking in three ways at once: host trust smoke under-reported revocation, HTTP/3 depended on tooling that this machine did not actually have, and eBPF JIT failure was still too blurry to act on. This pass turned each one into a sharper, user-visible contract.
- Boundary: Faz 1 is still not `tam uyumlu/exact`. Live trust and HTTP/3 smoke now pass, but host eBPF JIT still crashes before compile-only codegen completes, so interpreter-free runtime parity remains the last hard blocker in the remaining slice touched this turn.
- Evidence: `cargo build --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --features host_smoke --bin phase1_live_interop`; live host smoke with `PHASE1_SKIP_GRPC=1 PHASE1_SKIP_EBPF=1 PHASE1_SKIP_DOH=1 PHASE1_SKIP_DOT=1` (`trust` + inline `http3` passed); live host smoke with `PHASE1_SKIP_GRPC=1 PHASE1_SKIP_DOH=1 PHASE1_SKIP_DOT=1 PHASE1_SKIP_TRUST=1 PHASE1_SKIP_HTTP3=1 PHASE1_DEBUG_EBPF=1 PHASE1_SKIP_EBPF_RUN=1` (still crashed after `probe:before-compile`).

## 2026-03-16 | phase1-host-smoke-closure-pass | Host-safe serial logging, Windows JIT ABI preservation, and full Phase 1 live smoke closed the remaining host blockers
- Decision: `src/serial/uart.rs` now routes host-side serial logging to `stderr` instead of raw UART I/O. Host smoke binaries no longer execute privileged COM-port instructions when repository code uses `serial_println!`.
- Decision: `src/ebpf_jit.rs` now preserves `RDI` and `RSI` on Windows x64 host builds, matching the nonvolatile register contract that the JIT was violating while mapping BPF `R1/R2` onto those registers.
- Decision: `src/bin/phase1_live_interop.rs` corrected the host eBPF smoke program encoding, added operational TCP/HTTP timeout smoke, added IPv6 Router Advertisement to next-hop selection smoke, and added CNI `ADD/CHECK/DEL` lifecycle smoke. The DoH matrix remains quorum-based because Quad9 retired HTTP/1.1 DoH and still rejects this host's HTTP/1.1 requests with `400`.
- Decision: `docs/agent/network-capability-matrix.md` now records Phase 1 exact closure against the repository gate set using the full host smoke chain rather than the older "core verified, exact open" wording.
- Rationale: The remaining host blocker was not a platform impossibility. It was a chain of concrete defects: privileged host logging, Windows ABI corruption in JIT epilogue/prologue, and missing operational smoke for IPv6/CNI/TCP/HTTP. Once those were closed, the Phase 1 host evidence stopped being fragmented.
- Boundary: Provider-specific native parity still exists outside the Phase 1 gate, most visibly Quad9 DoH over HTTP/2. That is now a documented provider delta, not a silent success path or a live-smoke crash blocker.
- Evidence: `cargo build --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --features host_smoke --bin phase1_live_interop`; `.\target\x86_64-pc-windows-msvc\debug\phase1_live_interop.exe` with `EXIT:0` for `grpc + ebpf + doh + dot + trust + ops + ipv6 + cni + http3`.

## 2026-03-17 | win32-phase2-crt-automation-runtime-pass | Stateful kernel32/msvcrt runtime and real OLE automation dispatch
- Decision: `src/win32.rs` kernel32 compatibility state now carries a retained current directory and environment table instead of hardcoded answers. `CreateFileA` resolves relative Win32 paths through the retained cwd, `GetCurrentDirectoryA` / `SetCurrentDirectoryA` are stateful, `GetEnvironmentVariableA` / `SetEnvironmentVariableA` mutate a real case-insensitive map, and `GetCommandLineA` now returns a stable command-line buffer instead of `NULL`.
- Decision: `src/win32.rs` `msvcrt` stopped presenting broad API breadth with inert behavior across the main stdio/runtime paths. `_expand`, `fopen/fclose/fread/fwrite/fseek/ftell/feof/fgetc/fputc/fgets/fputs`, `fprintf/printf/sprintf/snprintf`, `time/clock/localtime/gmtime/asctime/ctime/strftime`, `getenv`, `atof/strtol/strtoul/strtod` now execute real retained state, kernel32-backed file handles, RTC-backed timestamps, and parser logic rather than default-returning.
- Decision: `src/win32.rs` OLE automation gained exported `DispGetIDsOfNames` and `DispInvoke` helpers that dispatch into the callee's real `IDispatch` vtable. `docs/agent/win32-parity-matrix.md` now records the CRT row as `Implemented + Partial` and adds an explicit `OLE automation` row so Phase 2 exactness no longer understates the landed runtime but also no longer hides the remaining type-info/type-library long tail.
- Rationale: The most concrete Phase 2 lie was no longer missing exports; it was stateful runtime calls that still returned canned values. That affected imported PE programs immediately through cwd/env/stdio/time behavior and also left OLE automation looking present in the export table while still lacking a truthful dispatch bridge.
- Boundary: Faz 2 is still not `tam uyumlu/exact`. `user32/gdi32` rows remain partial in the matrix, COM type-info/type-library and marshaling long-tail remain open, `WinHTTP/WinINet` cache/proxy/session parity remains open, and loader/unwind exactness still lacks forwarded/delay/bound import plus x64 unwind corpus closure. The local unit corpus for the new CRT/OLE paths was added in `src/win32.rs`, but `cargo test --lib` is still blocked by unrelated repo test errors in `src/rcu.rs`, `src/memory/fibonacci_pmm.rs`, `src/task/signal.rs`, and `src/personalization.rs`.
- Evidence: `cargo check --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib`; attempted `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib kernel32_environment_and_current_directory_are_stateful -- --exact` (blocked by unrelated existing lib-test compile failures outside the touched files).

## 2026-03-17 | win32-phase2-loader-gui-gdi-corpus-pass | Forwarded/delay import plumbing and retained GUI-GDI corpus are now stateful and locally pinned
- Decision: `src/pe_loader.rs` now parses delay-import descriptors into the same import graph as normal imports, records forwarded exports separately from direct export RVAs, and resolves forwarders recursively without holding the DLL mutex across the recursive lookup. Loader memory mapping now writes both normal IAT and delay-IAT slots through the same import-resolution path instead of treating delay imports as invisible metadata.
- Decision: `src/pe_loader.rs` gained local loader corpus for forwarder normalization and forwarded-export resolution. This pins the exact contract that Phase 2 previously described only in prose: `api-ms-*` style shim DLLs can now forward into concrete DLL exports and stay inside the truthful resolution path.
- Decision: `src/win32.rs` gained retained corpus for `TranslateAcceleratorA`, popup-menu message ordering, dialog focus/default-button routing, GDI selected-object lifetime, `SaveDC/RestoreDC`, and printer job queue commit semantics. The parity matrix now upgrades the GUI/GDI rows from raw `Partial` to `Implemented + Partial` so the repo stops underselling the stateful retained behavior while still keeping the missing Windows long-tail explicit.
- Rationale: After the CRT/OLE pass, Faz 2 still leaked exactness in two directions. Loader truthfulness still lagged behind modern forwarded/delay import PE shapes, and GUI/GDI retained behavior existed but lacked any local mechanical corpus to keep message ordering, selected-object lifetime, and printer queue semantics from regressing silently.
- Boundary: Faz 2 is still not `tam uyumlu/exact`. Bound import parity, full x64 unwind corpus, non-client/user32 long-tail, raster/path exactness, COM apartment/type-library parity, and WinHTTP/WinINet cache-proxy-session parity remain open. The new loader and GUI/GDI corpus lives in-tree, but `cargo test --lib` remains blocked by unrelated existing compile failures in `src/rcu.rs`, `src/memory/fibonacci_pmm.rs`, `src/task/signal.rs`, and `src/personalization.rs`.
- Evidence: `cargo check --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib`.

## 2026-03-17 | win32-phase2-internet-session-pass | WinHTTP and WinINet now retain session semantics instead of acting like stateless request shims
- Decision: `src/win32.rs` internet handle state now keeps retained session metadata: user-agent, access type, proxy strings, timeout buckets, request headers, response cursor, global cookie jar, and a small GET response cache. `WinHttpSetTimeouts`, `WinHttpQueryHeaders`, `WinHttpQueryDataAvailable`, `HttpQueryInfoA`, `InternetSetCookieA`, `InternetGetCookieA`, and `HttpSendRequestW` are now exported and wired into that retained state.
- Decision: `src/net/http.rs` gained an extra-header request path so WinHTTP/WinINet wrappers can carry retained `User-Agent`, request headers, and cookie headers into the real HTTP client rather than reporting session state that never influences the request path.
- Decision: retained-response query now exposes status code, status text, raw header block, content length, and `Set-Cookie` visibility through WinHTTP/WinINet query APIs. Successful GET requests populate an in-memory cache and response-side `Set-Cookie` headers feed the retained cookie jar.
- Rationale: After the GUI/GDI corpus pass, the biggest honest Phase 2 hole was no longer "can it send a request?" but "does it remember anything the way WinHTTP/WinINet do?" Before this pass, proxy strings, timeouts, cookies, query APIs, and request-state replay were effectively discarded even though the handles existed.
- Boundary: Faz 2 is still not `tam uyumlu/exact`. The retained proxy strings are now truthful state, but they do not yet drive a real upstream proxy transport. Schannel-grade trust policy, browser-grade cache semantics, COM apartment/type-library parity, and loader bound-import/x64-unwind long-tail are still open. The new WinHTTP/WinINet corpus was added in-tree, but `cargo test --lib` remains blocked by unrelated existing failures in `src/rcu.rs`, `src/memory/fibonacci_pmm.rs`, `src/task/signal.rs`, and `src/personalization.rs`.
- Evidence: `cargo check --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib`; attempted `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib winhttp_and_wininet_session_cookie_and_cache_are_stateful -- --exact` (blocked by unrelated existing lib-test compile failures outside touched files).

## 2026-03-17 | win32-phase2-unwind-com-apartment-pass | NTDLL unwind exports and COM apartment state now use real runtime metadata instead of catalog-only surface
- Decision: `src/win32.rs` COM apartment bookkeeping now stores retained model + refcount instead of a single raw `DWORD`. `CoInitialize` now maps to STA as on Windows, `CoInitializeEx` returns `S_FALSE` only for same-model reentry while incrementing the retained refcount, `CoUninitialize` decrements and only tears down the apartment at zero, and `CoGetApartmentType` is now exported against that retained state.
- Decision: `src/pe_loader.rs` now exposes `PeRuntimeFunction` with C ABI layout, and `src/win32_abi.rs` now exports `RtlLookupFunctionEntry` and `RtlVirtualUnwind` against the loaded PE exception directory instead of leaving unwind visibility at the prose level. Runtime-function lookup now snapshots the current process exception directory into a stable unwind cache and resolves control PCs against real PE ranges.
- Decision: `src/win32_abi.rs` also gained a local leaf-unwind corpus and runtime-function lookup corpus so unwind visibility no longer depends only on source inspection. The implementation covers leaf unwind and common x64 nonvolatile/stack-allocation unwind ops, while chained unwind and deep opcode coverage remain explicit open work.
- Rationale: After the internet-session pass, the biggest remaining Phase 2 lie was that COM apartmenting and x64 unwind were still more visible in exports and matrices than in actual runtime state. `CoInitialize` was even using MTA semantics by default, and the PE exception directory had no NTDLL-facing consumer.
- Boundary: Faz 2 is still not `tam uyumlu/exact`. `user32/gdi32` non-client and raster/path long-tail, COM type-library/marshaling parity, Schannel-grade proxy/trust parity, bound import exactness, and deep x64 unwind opcode/handler-data corpus remain open. `cargo test --lib` is still blocked by unrelated repo failures in `src/rcu.rs`, `src/memory/fibonacci_pmm.rs`, `src/task/signal.rs`, and `src/personalization.rs`.
- Evidence: `cargo check --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib`; attempted `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --lib com_apartment_model_and_refcount_are_stateful -- --exact` (blocked by unrelated existing lib-test compile failures outside touched files); `.\.qoder\skills\echos-architect\scripts\echos_gate.ps1 -Paths @('src\pe_loader.rs','src\win32.rs','src\win32_abi.rs')`.

- Decision: `TODO.md` Faz 9 was expanded from two generic headings into six concrete areas: physical memory/allocator core, virtual memory/address-space exactness, reclaim/pressure/recovery, topology/NUMA/hotplug, perf/latency/contention, and an explicit Faz 9 exactness closure note.

The phase now explicitly names PMM/allocator ownership, COW/map/unmap/TLB/hugepage semantics, reclaim/OOM policy, topology publication, rseq/per-cpu refresh, and perf/atomic/cache-line audits as closure gates.

## 2026-03-17 | phase5-driver-backlog-expansion | Faz 5 now treats driver support as a full exactness program instead of a narrow VirtIO/USB note
- Decision: `TODO.md` Faz 5 was expanded beyond VirtIO, USB, and a generic NIC/AHCI bucket. The driver roadmap now explicitly tracks NVMe queue/reset semantics, GPU/DRM/display publication, PCIe/MSI/IOMMU fabric behavior, and jail-class audio/wifi/bluetooth drivers with named exactness tasks.

Faz 5 closure now means exact hardware behavior across transport, DMA, interrupt, scanout/fence, and jail recovery boundaries instead of only "storage/network core exists."

## 2026-03-17 | phase5-linux-driver-onboarding-expansion | Faz 5 now explicitly covers Linux driver bind/load/run compatibility for declared profiles
- Decision: `TODO.md` Faz 5 gained a dedicated Linux driver onboarding block (`D-LNX-*`) covering supported-driver profile definition, `shim_layer` / `ironshim_bridge` DMA+IRQ+policy semantics, `dispatcher` bind/unbind and quarantine flow, ABI/API translation for major driver classes, lifecycle smoke, and shell/matrix truthfulness.

Faz 5 closure now explicitly requires echOS's declared Linux driver profiles to bind/load/run through the bridge rather than merely compile or appear in onboarding tables.

The repository already had IronShim, shim-layer, and dispatcher pieces, but without a named closure program it was still possible to overread them as universal Linux driver support. The new contract makes the target stronger and more honest at the same time: declared profiles must really run, and undeclared ones must stay explicitly unsupported.
- Boundary: This still does not claim "every Linux driver" will work when Faz 5 closes. The exact promise is narrower and truthful: echOS's declared Linux driver profiles must bind/load/run with real DMA/IRQ/policy behavior, and unsupported classes must be surfaced as unsupported instead of hand-waved.
- Evidence: `TODO.md` and `docs/agent/driver-capability-matrix.md` updated with Linux driver onboarding task groups and closure wording.
